     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Standalone validator for a change passport v1 document
     3	(schema/change-passport.v1.json; docs/PASSPORT.md is the plain-language
     4	guide; docs/NORTH-STAR-CHAIN.md, section "The change passport, in full", is
     5	the spec authority).
     6	
     7	WHY STANDALONE. A third party (BrotherSBE, a CI runner, anyone who never
     8	installed this repository) needs to check a passport file without pulling in
     9	this project's own tooling or a jsonschema library. So this file imports
    10	NOTHING from this repository and NOTHING beyond the Python standard library.
    11	It re-implements the handful of rules that actually matter by hand, rather
    12	than deferring to the JSON Schema document, because a schema-only check
    13	cannot express "field 4's items may be empty only when a justification is
    14	given" without machinery this file is deliberately avoiding.
    15	
    16	WHAT IT CHECKS, each one a NAMED reason on its own line when it fails:
    17	
    18	  1. "schema" equals the literal string "change-passport/v1".
    19	  2. The five consumer keys (whatWasDone, whoDidIt, whatWasRun,
    20	     whatWasNotEstablished, whereItCameFrom) are present and ANSWERED: a
    21	     non-empty list whose entries are non-blank strings. Matches the
    22	     consuming side's own answered() (BrotherSBE tools/sbe_onepager.py):
    23	     '' and None carry nothing, an empty list carries nothing.
    24	  3. Field 4's law: details.notEstablished.items is non-empty, OR items is
    25	     empty and details.notEstablished.noneClaimJustification is a non-blank
    26	     string. A literal claim that nothing was left unestablished needs its
    27	     own justification.
    28	  4. change.baseCommit and change.headCommit are present and hex-shaped
    29	     (7 to 40 hex characters).
    30	  5. details.who.accountableHuman is a non-blank string. Accountability is a
    31	     name, never a role.
    32	  6. Every entry in details.evidence carries an origin in {"local", "ci"}
    33	     and a timestamp.
    34	
    35	Exit codes: 0 and the line "VALID" when every check passes. 1 and one line
    36	per violation when the document is well-formed JSON but fails a rule.
    37	2 and one line naming the problem when the file cannot be read or is not
    38	valid JSON. A crash is never a pass: any unexpected exception is caught and
    39	reported as a named error at exit 2.
    40	
    41	Python 3.9, standard library only, no third-party schema library.
    42	
    43	Usage: python3 tools/bm_passport_validator.py <file>
    44	"""
    45	import json
    46	import re
    47	import sys
    48	
    49	_COMMIT_RE = re.compile(r"^[0-9a-fA-F]{7,40}$")
    50	_SCHEMA_VALUE = "change-passport/v1"
    51	
    52	#: The five keys the consumer (BrotherSBE tools/sbe_passport.py) reads
    53	#: directly off the deposit, in the chain's own order.
    54	_CONSUMER_KEYS = (
    55	    (1, "whatWasDone"),
    56	    (2, "whoDidIt"),
    57	    (3, "whatWasRun"),
    58	    (4, "whatWasNotEstablished"),
    59	    (5, "whereItCameFrom"),
    60	)
    61	
    62	
    63	def _answered_string(value):
    64	    return isinstance(value, str) and value.strip() != ""
    65	
    66	
    67	def _answered_list_of_strings(value):
    68	    """True when `value` is a non-empty list whose every entry is a
    69	    non-blank string. Mirrors the consuming side's answered() for a list:
    70	    non-empty AND every element itself answered."""
    71	    if not isinstance(value, list) or not value:
    72	        return False
    73	    return all(_answered_string(v) for v in value)
    74	
    75	
    76	def validate(doc):
    77	    """Return a list of violation strings. Empty list means valid. `doc`
    78	    is assumed to already be parsed JSON (any type); every shape check is
    79	    done here rather than assumed."""
    80	    problems = []
    81	    if not isinstance(doc, dict):
    82	        return ["the document is not a JSON object"]
    83	
    84	    # 1. schema key.
    85	    schema_value = doc.get("schema")
    86	    if schema_value != _SCHEMA_VALUE:
    87	        problems.append(
    88	            "schema must equal %r, got %r" % (_SCHEMA_VALUE, schema_value))
    89	
    90	    # 2. the five consumer keys, each answered-non-empty.
    91	    for number, key in _CONSUMER_KEYS:
    92	        value = doc.get(key)
    93	        if not _answered_list_of_strings(value):
    94	            problems.append(
    95	                "%s (field %d) must be a non-empty list of non-blank "
    96	                "strings, got %r" % (key, number, value))
    97	
    98	    details = doc.get("details")
    99	    details_is_object = isinstance(details, dict)
   100	    if not details_is_object:
   101	        problems.append("details must be an object, got %r" % (details,))
   102	
   103	    # 3. field 4's law, read from details.notEstablished rather than from
   104	    # the derived top-level whatWasNotEstablished list, because the law is
   105	    # about the SOURCE record, not about whether the derived line happened
   106	    # to be non-empty.
   107	    not_established = details.get("notEstablished") if details_is_object else None
   108	    if not isinstance(not_established, dict):
   109	        problems.append(
   110	            "details.notEstablished must be an object, got %r"
   111	            % (not_established,))
   112	    else:
   113	        items = not_established.get("items")
   114	        justification = not_established.get("noneClaimJustification")
   115	        if not isinstance(items, list):
   116	            problems.append(
   117	                "details.notEstablished.items must be a list, got %r"
   118	                % (items,))
   119	        elif not items:
   120	            if not _answered_string(justification):
   121	                problems.append(
   122	                    "details.notEstablished.items is empty and "
   123	                    "noneClaimJustification is not a non-blank string "
   124	                    "(%r); a literal claim that nothing was left "
   125	                    "unestablished needs its own justification"
   126	                    % (justification,))
   127	        elif not all(_answered_string(v) for v in items):
   128	            problems.append(
   129	                "details.notEstablished.items contains a blank or "
   130	                "non-string entry: %r" % (items,))
   131	
   132	    # 4. change identity: baseCommit / headCommit present and hex-shaped.
   133	    change = doc.get("change")
   134	    if not isinstance(change, dict):
   135	        problems.append("change must be an object, got %r" % (change,))
   136	    else:
   137	        for field in ("baseCommit", "headCommit"):
   138	            value = change.get(field)
   139	            if not _answered_string(value):
   140	                problems.append(
   141	                    "change.%s is required and must be a non-blank "
   142	                    "string, got %r" % (field, value))
   143	            elif not _COMMIT_RE.match(value.strip()):
   144	                problems.append(
   145	                    "change.%s is not hex-shaped (7 to 40 hex characters): "
   146	                    "%r" % (field, value))
   147	
   148	    # 5. accountableHuman: a name, never a role, never blank.
   149	    who = details.get("who") if details_is_object else None
   150	    if not isinstance(who, dict):
   151	        problems.append(
   152	            "details.who must be an object, got %r" % (who,))
   153	    else:
   154	        accountable = who.get("accountableHuman")
   155	        if not _answered_string(accountable):
   156	            problems.append(
   157	                "details.who.accountableHuman is required and must be a "
   158	                "non-blank name, got %r" % (accountable,))
   159	
   160	    # 6. every evidence entry carries origin in {local, ci} and a timestamp.
   161	    evidence = details.get("evidence") if details_is_object else None
   162	    if not isinstance(evidence, list):
   163	        problems.append(
   164	            "details.evidence must be a list, got %r" % (evidence,))
   165	    else:
   166	        for index, entry in enumerate(evidence):
   167	            if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   168	                problems.append(
   169	                    "details.evidence[%d] is not an object: %r"
   170	                    % (index, entry))
   171	                continue
   172	            origin = entry.get("origin")
   173	            if origin not in ("local", "ci"):
   174	                problems.append(
   175	                    "details.evidence[%d].origin must be \"local\" or "
   176	                    "\"ci\", got %r" % (index, origin))
   177	            if not _answered_string(entry.get("timestamp")):
   178	                problems.append(
   179	                    "details.evidence[%d].timestamp is required and must "
   180	                    "be a non-blank string, got %r"
   181	                    % (index, entry.get("timestamp")))
   182	
   183	    return problems
   184	
   185	
   186	def _load(path):
   187	    """Return (doc, error_line_or_None). Never raises: every failure to
   188	    read or parse becomes a plain-English line the caller prints at exit 2,
   189	    because an unreadable file and an invalid document are different facts
   190	    and only the second one is worth exit 1's "here is what to fix"."""
   191	    try:
   192	        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
   193	            text = fh.read()
   194	    except OSError as e:
   195	        return None, "could not read %s (%s: %s)" % (path, type(e).__name__, e)
   196	    try:
   197	        return json.loads(text), None
   198	    except ValueError as e:
   199	        return None, "%s is not valid JSON (%s)" % (path, e)
   200	
   201	
   202	def main(argv):
   203	    if len(argv) != 1:
   204	        sys.stderr.write(
   205	            "usage: python3 tools/bm_passport_validator.py <file>\n")
   206	        return 2
   207	    doc, error = _load(argv[0])
   208	    if error is not None:
   209	        sys.stdout.write(error + "\n")
   210	        return 2
   211	
   212	    problems = validate(doc)
   213	    if problems:
   214	        for line in problems:
   215	            sys.stdout.write(line + "\n")
   216	        return 1
   217	    sys.stdout.write("VALID\n")
   218	    return 0
   219	
   220	
   221	if __name__ == "__main__":
   222	    try:
   223	        sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
   224	    except Exception as exc:
   225	        sys.stdout.write("%s: %s\n" % (type(exc).__name__, exc))
   226	        sys.exit(2)

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Regression tests for tools/bm_passport.py, the change passport's producer
     3	half (docs/specs/2026-08-15-change-passport-seam.md in the BrotherSBE
     4	repository is the contract this tool fills a hole in).
     5	
     6	WHAT THIS SUITE IS ACTUALLY DEFENDING
     7	  Three rules from the contract, each load bearing and each proven by a test
     8	  whose guard was removed by hand to watch it go red:
     9	
    10	  1. A hollow value is never written. An empty string, an empty list, or a
    11	     null reads as absence on the consuming side, so a field this tool
    12	     cannot establish honestly must be OMITTED, never padded to look
    13	     filled.
    14	  2. Direction of travel: this tool writes the deposit and reads NOTHING
    15	     under .sbe/. A poisoned tasks.json sitting next to a healthy store
    16	     must change nothing about what this tool produces.
    17	  3. Absent, corrupt, and healthy stores are three DIFFERENT reported
    18	     states, and none of the three ever crashes the tool.
    19	
    20	Every fixture is a tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(), never the real project
    21	store. The CLI is invoked as a real subprocess so its argument parsing,
    22	exit codes, and stdout are exercised exactly as a caller would see them.
    23	
    24	ADDED: the `generate` verb (schema/change-passport.v1.json), a second,
    25	additive CLI shape on the same tool that writes the full five field
    26	passport rather than the three field deposit above. Its own section below
    27	covers: a round trip from a fixture store built through the Store write
    28	API (never a hand written store file), determinism across two generate
    29	calls sharing one --now, the standalone validator (tools/
    30	bm_passport_validator.py) against the canonical fixture and the three
    31	fixtures that each break exactly one rule, and a read only proof that
    32	BrotherSBE's own consumer (tools/sbe_passport.py in that sibling
    33	repository) reports fields 2 and 5 as CARRIED from a deposited canonical
    34	passport. That last test is guarded: it skips, by name, when the sibling
    35	repository is not present on the machine running this suite.
    36	
    37	Standard library only. Run: python3 tools/test_bm_passport.py
    38	"""
    39	import io
    40	import json
    41	import os
    42	import subprocess
    43	import sys
    44	import tempfile
    45	import unittest
    46	
    47	HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
    48	ROOT = os.path.dirname(HERE)
    49	TOOL_PATH = os.path.join(HERE, "bm_passport.py")
    50	VALIDATOR_PATH = os.path.join(HERE, "bm_passport_validator.py")
    51	SCHEMA_DIR = os.path.join(ROOT, "schema")
    52	FIXTURES_DIR = os.path.join(SCHEMA_DIR, "fixtures")
    53	CANONICAL_FIXTURE = os.path.join(FIXTURES_DIR, "change-passport.v1.canonical.json")
    54	INVALID_FIXTURES = {
    55	    "hollow field 4": os.path.join(FIXTURES_DIR, "invalid-hollow-field4.json"),
    56	    "missing accountable": os.path.join(FIXTURES_DIR, "invalid-missing-accountable.json"),
    57	    "evidence with no origin": os.path.join(FIXTURES_DIR, "invalid-evidence-no-origin.json"),
    58	}
    59	#: The sibling repository this project's own docs point at throughout
    60	#: (docs/NORTH-STAR-CHAIN.md, this tool's own docstring above). Read only:
    61	#: the contract proof test below never writes anything under this path.
    62	BROTHERSBE_ROOT = os.path.expanduser("~/Documents/BrotherSBE")
    63	
    64	
    65	def run_cli(*args, **kw):
    66	    """Invoke the CLI as a real subprocess, with BROTHERMODE_ROOT scrubbed
    67	    so a variable set on the developer's own machine can never leak into a
    68	    test that expects an explicit --root to decide the outcome. `env_over`
    69	    (a dict) is merged on top when a test needs to simulate an environment
    70	    where `git config user.name` has nothing to say."""
    71	    env = dict(os.environ)
    72	    env.pop("BROTHERMODE_ROOT", None)
    73	    env.update(kw.get("env_over") or {})
    74	    return subprocess.run(
    75	        [sys.executable, TOOL_PATH] + list(args),
    76	        stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
    77	        universal_newlines=True, cwd=ROOT, env=env)
    78	
    79	
    80	def write_text(directory, relpath, content="placeholder\n"):
    81	    path = os.path.join(directory, relpath)
    82	    parent = os.path.dirname(path)
    83	    if parent and not os.path.isdir(parent):
    84	        os.makedirs(parent)
    85	    with io.open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
    86	        handle.write(content)
    87	    return path
    88	
    89	
    90	def read_deposit(root, out=None):
    91	    path = out or os.path.join(root, ".sbe", "passport.json")
    92	    with io.open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
    93	        return json.load(handle)
    94	
    95	
    96	def _load_bm_store_module():
    97	    """Load bm_store.py the same way bm_passport.py does, so a test fixture
    98	    can build a real store without shelling out to a second CLI."""
    99	    import importlib.util
   100	    path = os.path.join(HERE, "bm_store.py")
   101	    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
   102	        "bm_store_for_passport_test", path)
   103	    mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
   104	    spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
   105	    return mod
   106	
   107	
   108	def claim_one(root, session_id="sess-abc", files=("a.py", "b.py")):
   109	    """Build a real, healthy store at `root` carrying one active claim.
   110	    Uses the writable Store directly (not the CLI): this is fixture setup,
   111	    not the thing under test."""
   112	    mod = _load_bm_store_module()
   113	    store = mod.Store(root, create=True)
   114	    try:
   115	        store.claim("unit-a", "ephemeral", objective="test fixture",
   116	                    files=list(files), owner="builder", session_id=session_id)
   117	    finally:
   118	        store.close()
   119	
   120	
   121	def claim_then_park(root, name, session_id, files):
   122	    """Add one claim and move it OUT of the active state, which is the state
   123	    a session's own record is in at the moment it closes and deposits. Parking
   124	    rather than completing on purpose: completing requires evidence, and this
   125	    fixture is about the STATE the producer reads, not about evidence."""
   126	    mod = _load_bm_store_module()
   127	    store = mod.Store(root, create=True)
   128	    try:
   129	        record = store.claim(name, "ephemeral", objective="test fixture",
   130	                             files=list(files), owner="builder",
   131	                             session_id=session_id)
   132	        # Record is a __slots__ object, not a dict: attribute access only.
   133	        # session_id must be passed back: the store's ownership guard refuses
   134	        # a cross-session move of an ACTIVE record, which is the single-writer
   135	        # rule doing its job, so the fixture parks as the same session.
   136	        store.transition(record.lifecycle_uuid, record.version, "parked",
   137	                         session_id=session_id)
   138	    finally:
   139	        store.close()

   142	#: An env override that makes `git config user.name` resolve to nothing,
   143	#: without touching this worktree's own git configuration: HOME points at
   144	#: an empty directory (no ~/.gitconfig) and the global/system config files
   145	#: are pointed at /dev/null, so only a repo-local .git/config (which these
   146	#: bare tempdir fixtures never have) could still answer. Applied to the
   147	#: SUBPROCESS environment only, via subprocess.run's own `env=`, never to
   148	#: this test runner's own environment or to any git command this file
   149	#: itself might run.
   150	def _no_git_identity_env(tmp):
   151	    empty_home = os.path.join(tmp, "empty-home")
   152	    os.makedirs(empty_home, exist_ok=True)
   153	    return {
   154	        "HOME": empty_home,
   155	        "GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL": os.devnull,
   156	        "GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM": os.devnull,
   157	    }

   840	class BrotherSBEContractProofTests(unittest.TestCase):
   841	    """Read only proof that the canonical fixture, deposited exactly where
   842	    the sibling repository expects it, makes BrotherSBE's own consumer
   843	    report fields 2 and 5 as CARRIED. Never writes into BROTHERSBE_ROOT:
   844	    the fixture store lives in a temp dir, and BrotherSBE's tool is only
   845	    ever invoked with --root pointed at that temp dir."""
   846	
   847	    def setUp(self):
   848	        sbe_passport = os.path.join(BROTHERSBE_ROOT, "tools", "sbe_passport.py")
   849	        if not os.path.isfile(sbe_passport):
   850	            self.skipTest(
   851	                "BrotherSBE is not present at %s on this machine, so the "
   852	                "contract proof cannot run; this is a skip, never a pass, "
   853	                "and never a fail" % sbe_passport)
   854	        self.sbe_passport = sbe_passport
   855	
   856	    def test_fields_two_and_five_are_carried_from_the_deposit(self):
   857	        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
   858	            sbe_dir = os.path.join(tmp, ".sbe")
   859	            os.makedirs(sbe_dir)
   860	            # Minimal tasks.json, mirroring BrotherSBE's own
   861	            # tools/test_sbe_passport.py fixture shape: an empty task list
   862	            # is a legal, usable registry for this seam, since fields 2 and
   863	            # 5 come from the deposit alone, never from tasks.
   864	            with io.open(os.path.join(sbe_dir, "tasks.json"), "w",
   865	                         encoding="utf-8") as fh:
   866	                json.dump({"tasks": []}, fh)
   867	            with io.open(CANONICAL_FIXTURE, "r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
   868	                canonical_bytes = fh.read()
   869	            with io.open(os.path.join(sbe_dir, "passport.json"), "w",
   870	                         encoding="utf-8") as fh:
   871	                fh.write(canonical_bytes)
   872	
   873	            result = subprocess.run(
   874	                [sys.executable, self.sbe_passport, "--root", tmp, "--json"],
   875	                stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
   876	                universal_newlines=True)
   877	        self.assertEqual(result.returncode, 0, result.stdout + result.stderr)
   878	        report = json.loads(result.stdout)
   879	        states = {f["number"]: f["state"] for f in report["fields"]}
   880	        self.assertEqual(states.get(2), "CARRIED",
   881	                         "field 2 (who did it) was not reported CARRIED: %s"
   882	                         % report)
   883	        self.assertEqual(states.get(5), "CARRIED",
   884	                         "field 5 (where it came from) was not reported "
   885	                         "CARRIED: %s" % report)

  5409	def withheld_marker(value):
  5410	    """The one marker every withheld field uses. Structurally honest (you can
  5411	    see the field is populated and how much of it there is) without
  5412	    reproducing a character of it."""
  5413	    return "[WITHHELD: %d chars of founder text]" % len(value)

  5416	def export_column(table, column, value):
  5417	    """THE CENTRAL WITHHOLDING POLICY. Given one (table, column, value) from
  5418	    the store, return what an ordinary, non-raw export may show for it.
  5419	
  5420	    Every JSON and text export that renders whole store rows goes through
  5421	    here, so the policy lives in exactly one place and a new column joins it
  5422	    by default rather than by remembering to add it. Order matters:
  5423	
  5424	      1. digest-shaped column      -> WITHHELD, whatever else it is listed as
  5425	      2. structurally safe column  -> value, unchanged
  5426	      3. id-shaped column          -> value if it really is id-shaped,
  5427	                                      WITHHELD if the caller put prose,
  5428	                                      a path or a key in it
  5429	      4. scrub-only column         -> secret-scrubbed, paths masked
  5430	      5. anything else that is text-> WITHHELD entirely
  5431	
  5432	    RELEASE CUT, 2026-07-29: the digest rule is FIRST, not last. FIX ROUND P3
  5433	    withheld digests inside dump() alone and reached them before the safe list;
  5434	    LOOP 11 moved the safe list in front of everything and listed two
  5435	    digest-shaped columns (learning_candidates.content_hash,
  5436	    learning_applications.task_fingerprint) as structural. Merging the two lanes
  5437	    with the safe check first would have re-exported those digests, which is the
  5438	    exact leak P3 closed, so the shape rule runs before the allowlist and covers
  5439	    every export rather than only dump. Git shas stay visible: they are named
  5440	    *_sha and *_head, not *_hash or *_fingerprint.
  5441	
  5442	    Raises RedactionUnavailable through redact_text rather than falling back
  5443	    to cleartext, exactly like every other exit in this file."""
  5444	    if isinstance(value, str) and value and column.endswith(_DUMP_DIGEST_SUFFIXES):
  5445	        # Withheld by name-shape: see _DUMP_DIGEST_SUFFIXES. A short answer
  5446	        # behind an unsalted digest is guessable, and redact_text cannot see a
  5447	        # digest at all.
  5448	        return "[WITHHELD: %d-char digest]" % len(value)
  5449	    if (table, column) in _DUMP_SAFE_COLUMNS:
  5450	        return value
  5451	    if not isinstance(value, str) or not value:
  5452	        return value
  5453	    if (table, column) in _DUMP_ID_SHAPED_COLUMNS:
  5454	        return value if is_id_shaped(value) else withheld_marker(value)
  5455	    if (table, column) in _DUMP_SCRUB_ONLY_COLUMNS:
  5456	        return mask_absolute_paths(redact_text(value))
  5457	    return withheld_marker(value)

  5460	def withhold_digest_columns(table, row):
  5461	    """One store row as a CLI's --json may print it, with every digest-shaped
  5462	    column run through export_column. Pure; returns a new dict.
  5463	
  5464	    WHY THIS EXISTS BESIDE export_column RATHER THAN INSTEAD OF IT. dump() renders
  5465	    whole rows and passes every column through the full policy. A CLI's --json
  5466	    prints ONE entity to the operator who just typed the command, so it
  5467	    deliberately shows columns dump withholds: a note's body is the text that
  5468	    operator typed a second earlier, and withholding it there would send people
  5469	    to --raw and lose the whole policy. The DIGEST rule is the one part that must
  5470	    hold on that surface too, because a digest is unreadable and therefore
  5471	    useless as diagnostics, while an unsalted digest of a line of a file is a
  5472	    confirmation oracle for text every other surface redacts.
  5473	
  5474	    FIX ROUND P2, 2026-07-30, reproduced against a real store before this
  5475	    existed: `bm_store dump` printed notes.anchor_line_hash as
  5760	    ("capability_receipts", "verification_state"),
  5761	    ("capability_receipts", "created_at"),
  5762	))
  5763	
  5764	
  5765	def _text_columns(conn, table):
  5766	    """Column names in `table` with TEXT storage affinity, read from the
  5767	    LIVE schema via PRAGMA table_info, not a hand-maintained list: a new
  5768	    column joins this the moment it exists in _DDL, with no other code
  5769	    change required for it to be redacted by default (GATE C)."""
  5770	    out = []
  5771	    for row in conn.execute("PRAGMA table_info(%s)" % table).fetchall():
  5772	        col_type = (row["type"] or "").upper()
  5773	        if any(kw in col_type for kw in ("CHAR", "TEXT", "CLOB")):
  5774	            out.append(row["name"])
  5775	    return out
  5776	
  5777	
  5778	def _export_row(conn, table, row_dict, list_fields=(), raw=False):
  5779	    """ONE row dict for `table`, redacted exactly as dump() redacts every
  5780	    row of that table: the same policy_cols computation over export_column
  5781	    (structural safe list first, digest-shape override on top), so a read
  5782	    accessor and dump() cannot drift by carrying this policy in two places
  5783	    (D-2, loop2 mechanical commands design 2026-08-01: "identical
  5784	    export_column policy as dump(); no new disclosure surface"). Every
  5785	    column named in `list_fields` (a shape's own LIST_FIELDS) is then
  5786	    decoded from its stored JSON text back into a Python list, but ONLY
  5787	    where redaction left it as literal JSON: a WITHHELD marker never
  5788	    parses, so json.loads on one always raises, and that failure is the
  5789	    signal a column was withheld, not an error to route around -- the
  5790	    marker string is left exactly as export_column produced it. Pure:
  5791	    returns a NEW dict, row_dict itself is untouched.
  5792	
  5793	    raw mirrors dump(raw=True)'s own gate exactly: True skips the
  5794	    export_column policy loop entirely, the identical "if raw: skip
  5795	    redaction" branch dump() takes, so a caller asking for raw gets every
  5796	    column exactly as the store holds it, no new disclosure surface beyond
  5797	    what dump(raw=True) already grants. The list_fields JSON decode below
  5798	    still runs either way: it is not a privacy decision (dump() has no
  5799	    concept of it at all, raw or not), only a representation one, and
  5800	    skipping it under raw would leave a caller-requested list as its own
  5801	    literal JSON text for no privacy reason."""
  5802	    out = dict(row_dict)
  5803	    if not raw:
  5804	        text_cols = _text_columns(conn, table)
  5805	        policy_cols = [c for c in text_cols
  5806	                       if (table, c) not in _DUMP_SAFE_COLUMNS
  5807	                       or c.endswith(_DUMP_DIGEST_SUFFIXES)]
  5808	        for col in policy_cols:
  5809	            if not out.get(col):
  5810	                continue
  5811	            out[col] = export_column(table, col, out[col])
  5812	    for col in list_fields:
  5813	        val = out.get(col)
  5814	        if isinstance(val, str):
  5815	            try:
  5816	                out[col] = json.loads(val)
  5817	            except ValueError:
  5818	                pass
  5819	    return out

  6594	
  6595	def _is_transient_busy_error(e):
  6596	    """True only for the two sqlite3.OperationalError messages that mean
  6597	    "another connection holds the lock right now, try again shortly" (fix-
  6598	    round 8, IMPORTANT: every OperationalError used to get the SAME "busy
  6599	    or locked, wait and retry" advice, so a genuinely missing table gave
  6600	    advice that can never work and hid CRITICAL A's own reproduction behind
  6601	    a comforting, wrong message). Every other OperationalError (a missing
  6602	    table or column, a disk I/O error, ...) means something is actually
  6603	    wrong, and deserves to say so."""
  6604	    msg = str(e).lower()
  6605	    return "database is locked" in msg or "database is busy" in msg
  6606	
  6607	
  6608	# FINDING 7: the exact conditions that may MOVE a founder's database.
  6609	#
  6610	# Before this, _is_transient_busy_error was a two-string allowlist and
  6611	# everything outside it was ASSUMED to be corruption, with no evidence that
  6612	# the file was damaged at all. A transient disk I/O error, a permission
  6613	# problem or a network-volume hiccup during the SessionStart hook's
  6614	# automatic `verify` was therefore enough to move a perfectly healthy store
  6615	# aside. The default is now inverted: quarantine happens only for a NAMED
  6616	# condition, and anything unrecognized reports without touching the file.
  6617	#
  6618	# QUARANTINES (evidence that the file itself is damaged):
  6619	#   * a cause that is not a sqlite3.Error at all. This module raises those
  6620	#     itself, and only after reading the file: zero length on disk, a table
  6621	#     genuinely absent from sqlite_master, a schema_version that does not
  6622	#     match. Those are findings, not guesses.
  6623	#   * type(cause) is exactly sqlite3.DatabaseError. Measured (Python 3.9.6,
  6624	#     SQLite 3.51.0): SQLITE_NOTADB raises DatabaseError('file is not a
  6625	#     database') and SQLITE_CORRUPT raises DatabaseError('database disk
  6626	#     image is malformed'); both arrive as the BASE class, never as an
  6627	#     OperationalError. That is the corruption class, by construction.
  6628	#   * a message naming corruption or a not-a-database file (below), so a
  6629	#     future SQLite that routes one of these through a subclass is still
  6630	#     caught.
  6631	#   * "no such table" / "no such column" from an OperationalError. This is
  6632	#     structural schema damage, not an environment hiccup: every table
  6633	#     named here is created by this module's own DDL, so sqlite reporting
  6634	#     one missing is evidence the schema was damaged (CRITICAL A,
  6635	#     fix-round 8, reproduced: drop the claims table, claim again, GRANTED
  6636	#     at exit 0). Kept deliberately.
  6637	#
  6638	# ONLY REPORTS NOW (the file is left exactly where it is, byte for byte):
  6639	#   "database is locked" / "database is busy" (already refused 'db-busy'
  6640	#   before reaching here), "disk I/O error", "unable to open database
  6641	#   file", "attempt to write a readonly database", "database or disk is
  6642	#   full", "not authorized", permission errors, and every other
  6643	#   OperationalError, ProgrammingError, DataError, InternalError or
  6644	#   NotSupportedError this list does not name.
  6645	_CORRUPTION_MESSAGE_FRAGMENTS = (
  6646	    "database disk image is malformed",
  6647	    "file is not a database",
  6648	    "file is encrypted or is not a database",
  6649	    "malformed database schema",
  6650	    "unsupported file format",
  6651	)
  6652	_SCHEMA_DAMAGE_MESSAGE_FRAGMENTS = ("no such table", "no such column")
  6653	
  6654	
  6655	def _quarantine_is_warranted(cause):
  6656	    """True only when `cause` is evidence the store FILE is damaged. See the
  6657	    note above for the exact conditions on each side of the line. Fails
  6658	    SAFE: an unrecognized error returns False, so the destructive action
  6659	    requires a positive reason rather than the absence of one."""
  6660	    if not isinstance(cause, sqlite3.Error):
  6661	        return True
  6662	    if type(cause) is sqlite3.DatabaseError:
  6663	        return True
  6664	    msg = str(cause).lower()
  6665	    if any(frag in msg for frag in _CORRUPTION_MESSAGE_FRAGMENTS):
  6666	        return True
  6667	    if isinstance(cause, sqlite3.OperationalError):
  6668	        return any(frag in msg for frag in _SCHEMA_DAMAGE_MESSAGE_FRAGMENTS)
  6669	    return False
  6670	
  6671	
  6672	def _exec(store, sql, params=()):
  6673	    """The ONE place any SQL statement runs against a Store's connection
  6674	    (GATE 4): Store.__init__ only probed the schema at OPEN time, so damage
  6675	    on a later page opened fine and leaked a raw sqlite3.DatabaseError out
  6676	    of claim(), dump(), and verify(), unquarantined, exit 1. Every query in
  6677	    this module routes through here so the same split applies everywhere:
  6678	    a transient busy/locked OperationalError refuses 'db-busy'; any other
  6679	    DatabaseError goes to the store's quarantine entry point, which decides
  6680	    (FINDING 7) whether the evidence names a damaged FILE, in which case a
  6681	    writable store moves it aside, or an environment failure, in which case
  6682	    it is reported and the file is left untouched. A read-only store never
  6683	    moves anything either way. Structural schema damage such as a dropped
  6684	    table still quarantines (CRITICAL A: this is also the backstop for a
  6685	    table dropped from under an already-open connection, not only one
  6686	    caught at the next fresh open). sqlite3.IntegrityError is
  6687	    let through UNCHANGED: a unique-constraint hit is caller-shaped, not
  6688	    corruption (see transition()'s 'name-active' handling, GATE 6), and the
  6689	    caller is better placed to turn it into a named refusal than this is."""
  6690	    try:
  6691	        return store.conn.execute(sql, params)
  6692	    except sqlite3.IntegrityError:
  6693	        raise
  6694	    except sqlite3.OperationalError as e:
  6695	        if not _is_transient_busy_error(e):
  6696	            store._quarantine_and_raise(e)
  6697	        raise OwnershipRefused(
  6698	            "db-busy",
  6699	            "the store at %s is busy or locked (%s); wait a moment and retry."
  6700	            % (store.path, e))
  6701	    except sqlite3.DatabaseError as e:
  6702	        store._quarantine_and_raise(e)
  6703	
  6704	
  6705	# Every Store/ReadOnlyStore whose sqlite handle is currently open. This is the
  6706	# MECHANICAL STOP for the leak class behind the Windows CI failure (run 18,
  6707	# commit 7c2e0ec): that round fixed 12 known call sites by hand, and a
  6708	# hand-fixed call site is not a fixed class, because the 13th site is written
  6709	# by someone who never read the fix. The suite asserts this set is empty at
  6710	# teardown, so ANY future site that opens a store and abandons it fails the
  6711	# suite on every platform, not only on the one that happens to lock files.
  6712	#
  6713	# A WeakSet rather than a list, deliberately: a store that has been garbage
  6714	# collected no longer holds an OS handle (CPython closes the sqlite connection
  6715	# when it finalizes), so counting it would report a leak that cannot hurt
  6716	# anyone. What broke Windows was a store still REFERENCED and still open when
  6717	# the directory was removed, and that is exactly what stays visible here.
  6718	_OPEN_STORES = weakref.WeakSet()
  6719	
  6720	# Discipline tracking, OFF by default. _OPEN_STORES tracks stores that are
  6721	# still alive, which turned out to be the wrong question: a store abandoned
  6722	# inside a test is freed the moment the test function returns, so by the time
  6723	# any external checker looks, the set is empty and the leak reports clean.
  6724	# That was measured, not assumed: a deliberately reinjected leak passed a
  6725	# liveness-based check.
  6726	#
  6727	# What matters is whether a store was ever closed, not whether it is still
  6728	# alive when someone asks. _UNCLOSED holds a STRONG reference, so an abandoned
  6729	# store stays visible after collection would have hidden it. It is opt-in
  6730	# precisely because strong references retain sqlite connections: the test
  6731	# runner sets _TRACK_UNCLOSED, and the shipping CLI never does, so normal use
  6732	# keeps exactly the memory profile it had before.
  6733	_TRACK_UNCLOSED = False
  6734	_UNCLOSED = set()
  6735

  6737	class Store(object):
  6738	    """One open connection to <root>/.brothermode/store.sqlite3. Every
  6739	    ownership mutation runs inside exactly one BEGIN IMMEDIATE ... COMMIT
  6740	    (see _transaction), so a reader never observes a half-written record and
  6741	    two writers on the same file never interleave."""
  6742

  6744	        """busy_timeout_ms defaults to the spec's 5000; exposed as a keyword
  6745	        so a test can force a near-instant "database is locked" without a

 13606	        # `removed` describes rows that are all still there. That is the
 13607	        # point: the numbers are the real purge's own, computed by the real

 13635	        """ONE project row by id, or None if no such project."""
 13636	        row = _exec(self, "SELECT * FROM projects WHERE project_id=?",
 13637	                    (project_id,)).fetchone()
 13638	        if row is None:

 13640	        S = _schema()

 13644	    def list_projects(self, raw=False, include_system=False):
 13645	        """Every project A FOUNDER STARTED, oldest first (created_at,
 13646	        project_id tie break). Empty list if the store has none.
 13647	
 13648	        include_system=False, the default, hides the rows the product
 13649	        registered for its own bookkeeping (SYSTEM_PROJECT_IDS, whose
 13650	        comment carries the reasoning and the reproduction). Every caller
 13651	        of this method is asking a founder-facing question, and a row the
 13652	        product filed its own alerts under is not an answer to any of
 13653	        them. Default-exclude rather than opt-out on purpose: a new caller
 13654	        that never heard of the distinction gets the founder-facing
 13655	        answer, which is the one it wanted, instead of silently counting
 13656	        bookkeeping as a project.
 13657	
 13658	        include_system=True returns every row, for a caller asking about
 13659	        the TABLE rather than about the founder's work. Nothing in the
 13660	        product needs it today: dump() and verify() read the projects
 13661	        table directly and are untouched by this."""
 13662	        rows = _exec(self,
 13663	            "SELECT * FROM projects ORDER BY created_at ASC, "
 13664	            "project_id ASC").fetchall()
 13665	        S = _schema()
 13666	        list_fields = S.Project.LIST_FIELDS
 13667	        return [_export_row(self.conn, "projects", dict(r),
 13668	                             list_fields, raw=raw) for r in rows
 13669	                if include_system
 13670	                or r["project_id"] not in SYSTEM_PROJECT_IDS]

 13672	    def list_tasks(self, project_id, status=None, raw=False):
 13673	        """Every task in `project_id`, INSERTION order: tasks carries no
 13674	        timestamp of its own to order by, and task_id (a random hex uuid)
 13675	        does not preserve the order tasks were added in either, which used
 13676	        to make cmd_next's own "earliest created" claim false (C6,
 13677	        release-closure loop2 refuter fixes). sqlite's own rowid is the
 13678	        one column every ordinary table (task_id is TEXT, not INTEGER, so
 13679	        it is never a rowid alias) already carries for free and that DOES
 13680	        grow monotonically with each INSERT, so ordering by it is
 13681	        insertion order without adding a column. `status` narrows to ONE
 13682	        of schema.py's ten legal values when given; an unrecognised status
 13683	        simply matches nothing, the same advisory degrade as a missing
 13684	        project_id."""
 13685	        S = _schema()
 13686	        if status is None:
 13687	            rows = _exec(self,
 13688	                "SELECT * FROM tasks WHERE project_id=? ORDER BY rowid ASC",
 13689	                (project_id,)).fetchall()
 13690	        else:
 13691	            rows = _exec(self,
 13692	                "SELECT * FROM tasks WHERE project_id=? AND status=? "
 13693	                "ORDER BY rowid ASC", (project_id, status)).fetchall()
 13694	        return [_export_row(self.conn, "tasks", dict(r), S.Task.LIST_FIELDS,
 13695	                             raw=raw) for r in rows]

 13716	        touches them either way."""

 13754	    def list_capability_receipts(self, project_id, task_id=None,
 13755	                                  capability_name=None, raw=False):

 13759	        oldest-first is that row's own history in the order it was made.
 13760	
 13761	        `task_id` and `capability_name` narrow the read the same way
 13762	        list_tasks narrows on status: both optional, both exact match,
 13763	        neither changes the base query when omitted, and both may be
 13764	        combined with each other and with `project_id`."""

 13766	        params = [project_id]

 13769	            params.append(task_id)
 13770	        if capability_name is not None:
 13771	            clauses.append("capability_name=?")
 13772	            params.append(capability_name)
 13773	        rows = _exec(self,
 13774	            "SELECT * FROM capability_receipts WHERE %s "
 13775	            "ORDER BY created_at ASC, receipt_id ASC"
 13776	            % " AND ".join(clauses),
 13777	            tuple(params)).fetchall()
 13778	        return [_export_row(self.conn, "capability_receipts", dict(r),
 13779	                             _CAPABILITY_RECEIPT_LIST_FIELDS, raw=raw)
 13780	                for r in rows]

 13845	    # -- the Memory Sentinel (schema 13, section 3 of
 13846	    # docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-02-memory-sentinel-phase1-design.md,
 13847	    # 2026-08-02) -------------------------------------------------------
 13848	    #
 13849	    # Twelve methods over the four sentinel tables. `actor` is the same
 13850	    # small dict every LOOP 1 method above takes, and it is here for the
 13851	    # same job: each method that CHANGES state and takes an actor writes
 13852	    # its row and its attribution row in ONE self._transaction(), both or
 13853	    # neither. That is not a choice made here, it is this module's standing
 13854	    # rule (every one of the twelve actor-taking methods above does it) and
 13855	    # the design points at upsert_project by name for exactly this.
 13856	    # mark_surfaced and judge_intervention take no actor and write no
 13857	    # attribution: mark_surfaced only bumps a counter on rows whose
 13858	    # creation is already attributed and whose surfacing the ledger row
 13859	    # itself names, and judge_intervention records its own grader in
 13860	    # judged_by, on the row it grades.
 13861	    #
 13862	    # Free text is stored EXACTLY as given, never scrubbed on the way in.
 13863	    # Same as every LOOP 1 method above, and deliberately unlike add_note,
 13864	    # for two reasons the design forces: the reminder the sentinel prints
 13865	    # has to be the verbatim sentence somebody recorded, or the ledger
 13866	    # grades text nobody wrote; and the export side already withholds all
 13867	    # of it, because dump() is default-deny read from the LIVE schema, so
 13868	    # these four tables' prose columns are withheld the day they exist
 13869	    # without one entry being added to any allowlist.
 13870	    #
 13871	    # The reads below return PLAIN row dicts (like list_notes), not
 13872	    # _export_row dicts: they feed the selector and the reminder renderer
 13873	    # inside this process, and a selector handed WITHHELD placeholders
 13874	    # would be matching tokens against the redactor's own output. dump()
 13875	    # remains the export surface and still withholds every one of these
 13876	    # columns.
 13877	
 13878	    def _require_sentinel_project(self, project_id):
 13879	        """Refuse a sentinel write against a project that does not exist,
 13880	        naming it (SBE10 fix, 2026-08-15).
 13881	
 13882	        The four sentinel tables deliberately carry no REFERENCES clause,
 13883	        so sqlite accepted any string at all as a project_id and the row
 13884	        landed somewhere nothing could reach: purge_project refuses an
 13885	        unknown project outright, so prose filed under a typo could never
 13886	        be purged by the command that exists to purge it. The attribution
 13887	        row each of these writes also produces made the same store's own
 13888	        verify() report a dangling project reference.
 13889	
 13890	        tools/bm_sentinel.py already ran this exact check in front of its
 13891	        four CLI commands, and its _require_project docstring carries the
 13892	        reproduction that put it there. That guard protected the CLI and
 13893	        nothing else. This is the same refusal moved to where every caller
 13894	        routes through it, so the CLI's own check now fires first and this
 13895	        one catches everybody else. Called INSIDE each write's transaction,
 13896	        like add_capability_receipt's own project check, so a refusal
 13897	        leaves nothing behind."""
 13898	        row = _exec(self, "SELECT project_id FROM projects WHERE "
 13899	                    "project_id=?", (project_id,)).fetchone()
 13900	        if row is None:
 13901	            raise OwnershipRefused(
 13902	                "not-found",
 13903	                "no project %r in this store; a sentinel memory attaches to "
 13904	                "a project, and one written against a project that does not "
 13905	                "exist can never be purged with it" % (project_id,))
 13906	
 13907	    def add_knowledge(self, project_id, kind, content, source, session_id,
 13908	                       actor):
 13909	        """Record ONE verified fact, with its attribution event
 13910	        ('sentinel.knowledge.added'), in ONE transaction. Returns the new
 13911	        id.
 13912	
 13913	        `kind` is refused, never coerced, when it is outside
 13914	        SENTINEL_KNOWLEDGE_KINDS: the message names the field and the whole
 13915	        allowed set, because a kind quietly rewritten to something legal is
 13916	        a kind nobody can audit afterwards. The row starts life active,
 13917	        never surfaced (surface_count 0, last_surfaced_at NULL), which is
 13918	        what makes 'has this been said yet' answerable at all."""
 13919	        _sentinel_enum("kind", kind, SENTINEL_KNOWLEDGE_KINDS)
 13920	        memory_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
 13921	        with self._transaction():
 13922	            self._require_sentinel_project(project_id)
 13923	            _exec(self,
 13924	                  "INSERT INTO sentinel_knowledge (id, project_id, "
 13925	                  "session_id, kind, content, source, created_at, "
 13926	                  "last_surfaced_at, surface_count, active, superseded_by) "
 13927	                  "VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?,?,NULL,0,1,NULL)",
 13928	                  (memory_id, project_id, session_id, kind, content, source,
 13929	                   now_iso()))
 13930	            self._write_attribution(
 13931	                project_id, None, "sentinel.knowledge.added", actor,
 13932	                action="add_knowledge", evidence_ref=memory_id)
 13933	        return memory_id
 13934	
 13935	    def add_procedural(self, project_id, attempt, outcome, diagnosis,
 13936	                        session_id, actor):
 13937	        """Record ONE thing that was tried and what happened, with its
 13938	        attribution event ('sentinel.procedural.added'), in ONE
 13939	        transaction. Returns the new id.
 13940	
 13941	        This is the table that exists to stop a repeated failed attempt, so
 13942	        `attempt` is stored in the words that would be recognised again
 13943	        rather than summarised: the selector matches tokens against it.
 13944	        `outcome` is refused when outside SENTINEL_PROCEDURAL_OUTCOMES;
 13945	        `diagnosis` is nullable because 'it failed and nobody yet knows
 13946	        why' is a real and useful state, and inventing a diagnosis to fill
 13947	        the column would be worse than leaving it empty."""
 13948	        _sentinel_enum("outcome", outcome, SENTINEL_PROCEDURAL_OUTCOMES)
 13949	        memory_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
 13950	        with self._transaction():
 13951	            self._require_sentinel_project(project_id)
 13952	            _exec(self,
 13953	                  "INSERT INTO sentinel_procedural (id, project_id, "
 13954	                  "session_id, attempt, outcome, diagnosis, created_at, "
 13955	                  "last_surfaced_at, surface_count, active) "
 13956	                  "VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?,?,NULL,0,1)",
 13957	                  (memory_id, project_id, session_id, attempt, outcome,
 13958	                   diagnosis, now_iso()))
 13959	            self._write_attribution(
 13960	                project_id, None, "sentinel.procedural.added", actor,
 13961	                action="add_procedural", evidence_ref=memory_id)
 13962	        return memory_id
 13963	
 13964	    def set_status(self, project_id, summary, open_risks, session_id,
 13965	                    actor):
 13966	        """Append ONE status row, with its attribution event
 13967	        ('sentinel.status.set'), in ONE transaction. Returns the new id.
 13968	
 13969	        APPEND-ONLY despite the name: 'set' never edits or replaces a prior
 13970	        row, it adds the next one, so the watcher's view of progress keeps
 13971	        its own history. latest_status reads the newest.
 13972	
 13973	        This table is PRIVATE by design. Nothing here is ever injected into
 13974	        the working agent's context and the selector is forbidden from
 13975	        reading it, which is why no method below returns a status row
 13976	        anywhere near a reminder."""
 13977	        status_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
 13978	        with self._transaction():
 13979	            self._require_sentinel_project(project_id)
 13980	            _exec(self,
 13981	                  "INSERT INTO sentinel_status (id, project_id, session_id, "
 13982	                  "summary, open_risks, created_at) VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?)",
 13983	                  (status_id, project_id, session_id, summary, open_risks,
 13984	                   now_iso()))
 13985	            self._write_attribution(
 13986	                project_id, None, "sentinel.status.set", actor,
 13987	                action="set_status", evidence_ref=status_id)
 13988	        return status_id
 13989	
 13990	    def latest_status(self, project_id):
 13991	        """The newest status row for `project_id` as a plain dict, or None
 13992	        when the watcher has never written one.
 13993	
 13994	        created_at DESC then rowid DESC: now_iso() is second-precision, so
 13995	        two statuses written inside the same second compare EQUAL on
 13996	        created_at alone and 'the latest' would be whichever row sqlite
 13997	        happened to hand back first. rowid is the one column every ordinary
 13998	        table already carries that grows with each INSERT, so it settles
 13999	        that tie as insertion order, the same reason list_tasks orders by
 14000	        it."""
 14001	        row = _exec(self,
 14002	            "SELECT * FROM sentinel_status WHERE project_id=? "
 14003	            "ORDER BY created_at DESC, rowid DESC LIMIT 1",
 14004	            (project_id,)).fetchone()
 14005	        if row is None:
 14006	            return None
 14007	        return dict(row)
 14008	
 14009	    def active_knowledge(self, project_id, kinds=None):
 14010	        """Every ACTIVE knowledge row for `project_id`, oldest first
 14011	        (created_at, then rowid for rows written in the same second).
 14012	
 14013	        `kinds` narrows to a set of kinds, and every kind in it is checked
 14014	        against SENTINEL_KNOWLEDGE_KINDS first: a typo in a filter that
 14015	        simply returned an empty list is indistinguishable from a project
 14016	        that recorded nothing, which is the exact shape of silent wrongness
 14017	        this design refuses. Retired rows (active 0) never come back from
 14018	        here, whatever the filter says."""
 14019	        sql = "SELECT * FROM sentinel_knowledge WHERE project_id=? AND active=1"
 14020	        params = [project_id]
 14021	        if kinds is not None:
 14022	            kinds = tuple(kinds)
 14023	            for kind in kinds:
 14024	                _sentinel_enum("kind", kind, SENTINEL_KNOWLEDGE_KINDS)
 14025	            if not kinds:
 14026	                return []
 14027	            sql += " AND kind IN (%s)" % ",".join("?" * len(kinds))
 14028	            params.extend(kinds)
 14029	        sql += " ORDER BY created_at ASC, rowid ASC"
 14030	        return [dict(r) for r in _exec(self, sql, tuple(params)).fetchall()]
 14031	
 14032	    def active_procedural(self, project_id, outcomes=None):
 14033	        """Every ACTIVE procedural row for `project_id`, oldest first, with
 14034	        the same contract active_knowledge carries: `outcomes` is checked
 14035	        against SENTINEL_PROCEDURAL_OUTCOMES before it filters anything,
 14036	        and a retired row never comes back."""
 14037	        sql = "SELECT * FROM sentinel_procedural WHERE project_id=? AND active=1"
 14038	        params = [project_id]
 14039	        if outcomes is not None:
 14040	            outcomes = tuple(outcomes)
 14041	            for outcome in outcomes:
 14042	                _sentinel_enum("outcome", outcome,
 14043	                                SENTINEL_PROCEDURAL_OUTCOMES)
 14044	            if not outcomes:
 14045	                return []
 14046	            sql += " AND outcome IN (%s)" % ",".join("?" * len(outcomes))
 14047	            params.extend(outcomes)
 14048	        sql += " ORDER BY created_at ASC, rowid ASC"
 14049	        return [dict(r) for r in _exec(self, sql, tuple(params)).fetchall()]
 14050	
 14051	    def retire_memory(self, table, memory_id, superseded_by, actor):
 14052	        """Retire ONE memory (active 1 to 0), with its attribution event
 14053	        ('sentinel.memory.retired'), in ONE transaction. The row STAYS;
 14054	        nothing here deletes anything. True when a row actually moved,
 14055	        False when there is no such id or it was already retired, so a
 14056	        caller can tell 'I retired it' from 'there was nothing to retire'.
 14057	
 14058	        `table` goes through _sentinel_table BEFORE any SQL is built, and
 14059	        the two UPDATE statements below are separate literals rather than
 14060	        one interpolated string, so no caller text reaches a statement at
 14061	        all.
 14062	
 14063	        superseded_by is written only for sentinel_knowledge, because that
 14064	        is the only one of the two tables the design gives the column to
 14065	        (sections 2.1 and 2.2). Asking to supersede a procedural memory is
 14066	        therefore REFUSED by name rather than accepted and dropped on the
 14067	        floor: a caller that believes it recorded which attempt replaced
 14068	        which, when nothing was recorded, is worse off than one that got an
 14069	        error."""
 14070	        table = _sentinel_table(table)
 14071	        if table == "sentinel_procedural" and superseded_by:
 14072	            raise ValueError(
 14073	                "sentinel_procedural has no superseded_by column, so "
 14074	                "superseded_by=%r cannot be recorded; retire it with "
 14075	                "superseded_by=None, or record the replacement as its own "
 14076	                "procedural row" % (superseded_by,))
 14077	        retired = False
 14078	        with self._transaction():
 14079	            row = _exec(self,
 14080	                "SELECT project_id, active FROM %s WHERE id=?" % table,
 14081	                (memory_id,)).fetchone()
 14082	            if row is not None and row["active"]:
 14083	                if table == "sentinel_knowledge":
 14084	                    _exec(self,
 14085	                          "UPDATE sentinel_knowledge SET active=0, "
 14086	                          "superseded_by=? WHERE id=? AND active=1",
 14087	                          (superseded_by, memory_id))
 14088	                else:
 14089	                    _exec(self,
 14090	                          "UPDATE sentinel_procedural SET active=0 "
 14091	                          "WHERE id=? AND active=1", (memory_id,))
 14092	                self._write_attribution(
 14093	                    row["project_id"], None, "sentinel.memory.retired",
 14094	                    actor, action="retire_memory", evidence_ref=memory_id)
 14095	                retired = True
 14096	        return retired
 14097	
 14098	    def mark_surfaced(self, table, memory_ids):
 14099	        """Stamp last_surfaced_at and bump surface_count on the named
 14100	        memories. Returns how many rows were actually updated, which is not
 14101	        always len(memory_ids): an id that names nothing updates nothing,
 14102	        and saying so is the point of returning a count rather than None.
 14103	
 14104	        `table` goes through the same _sentinel_table whitelist
 14105	        retire_memory uses, before any SQL is built. memory_ids accepts a
 14106	        list of ids or ONE comma-separated string (see _sentinel_id_list);
 14107	        a bare string is never treated as a sequence of characters.
 14108	
 14109	        No actor and no attribution row here, unlike the writes above: this
 14110	        bumps a counter on rows whose creation is already attributed, and
 14111	        the intervention row written beside it already names exactly which
 14112	        ids were surfaced and why."""
 14113	        table = _sentinel_table(table)
 14114	        ids = _sentinel_id_list(memory_ids)
 14115	        if not ids:
 14116	            return 0
 14117	        ts = now_iso()
 14118	        with self._transaction():
 14119	            cursor = _exec(self,
 14120	                "UPDATE %s SET last_surfaced_at=?, "
 14121	                "surface_count=surface_count+1 WHERE id IN (%s)"
 14122	                % (table, ",".join("?" * len(ids))),
 14123	                tuple([ts] + ids))
 14124	            updated = cursor.rowcount
 14125	        return updated
 14126	
 14127	    def record_intervention(self, project_id, trigger, decision, memory_ids,
 14128	                             reminder, reason, session_id, actor):
 14129	        """Append ONE calibration-ledger row, with its attribution event
 14130	        ('sentinel.intervention.recorded'), in ONE transaction. Returns the
 14131	        new id.
 14132	
 14133	        EVERY decision writes a row, including every silence. That is the
 14134	        whole design: a silence recorded with its reason is evidence, while
 14135	        a silence recorded as an absence is indistinguishable from the
 14136	        sentinel never having run. `trigger` and `decision` are refused
 14137	        when outside SENTINEL_TRIGGERS and SENTINEL_DECISIONS.
 14138	
 14139	        memory_ids is stored comma-separated, and the empty string (never
 14140	        NULL) when nothing was injected, so 'silent' rows all have the same
 14141	        shape. The row lands unjudged; judge_intervention grades it later,
 14142	        and intervention_stats counts what is still ungraded rather than
 14143	        treating ungraded as neutral."""
 14144	        _sentinel_enum("trigger", trigger, SENTINEL_TRIGGERS)
 14145	        _sentinel_enum("decision", decision, SENTINEL_DECISIONS)
 14146	        intervention_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
 14147	        with self._transaction():
 14148	            self._require_sentinel_project(project_id)
 14149	            _exec(self,
 14150	                  "INSERT INTO sentinel_interventions (id, project_id, "
 14151	                  "session_id, trigger, decision, memory_ids, reminder, "
 14152	                  "reason, created_at, judged, judged_at, judged_by) "
 14153	                  "VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?,'unjudged',NULL,NULL)",
 14154	                  (intervention_id, project_id, session_id, trigger,
 14155	                   decision, ",".join(_sentinel_id_list(memory_ids)),
 14156	                   reminder, reason, now_iso()))
 14157	            self._write_attribution(
 14158	                project_id, None, "sentinel.intervention.recorded", actor,
 14159	                action="record_intervention", reason=reason or "",
 14160	                evidence_ref=intervention_id)
 14161	        return intervention_id
 14162	
 14163	    def judge_intervention(self, intervention_id, judged, judged_by):
 14164	        """Grade ONE intervention. True when a row was graded, False when
 14165	        no such id exists.
 14166	
 14167	        `judged` is refused when outside SENTINEL_JUDGEMENTS; 'unjudged' is
 14168	        in that set on purpose, because the column's own default is
 14169	        'unjudged' and a grader who wants to take a grade BACK should be
 14170	        able to say so in the same vocabulary rather than by writing an
 14171	        undeclared value. judged_at is stamped whichever value lands, so
 14172	        the row always says when it was last touched.
 14173	
 14174	        No actor and no attribution row: judged_by is the grader, recorded
 14175	        on the row itself, which is where a ledger reader looks for it."""
 14176	        _sentinel_enum("judged", judged, SENTINEL_JUDGEMENTS)
 14177	        with self._transaction():
 14178	            cursor = _exec(self,
 14179	                "UPDATE sentinel_interventions SET judged=?, judged_at=?, "
 14180	                "judged_by=? WHERE id=?",
 14181	                (judged, now_iso(), judged_by, intervention_id))
 14182	            updated = cursor.rowcount
 14183	        return updated == 1
 14184	
 14185	    def intervention_stats(self, project_id):
 14186	        """The calibration counts for `project_id`: total, injected,
 14187	        silent, useful, noise, unjudged, and useful_ratio.
 14188	
 14189	        useful_ratio is None, NEVER 0.0, when nothing has been judged. A
 14190	        ratio computed over zero judgements is a number that looks like
 14191	        measurement and is not one, and 0.0 in particular reads as 'we
 14192	        measured, and it was useless'. The caller renders None as NO-DATA.
 14193	
 14194	        The SUMs come back NULL from sqlite when the project has no rows at
 14195	        all, so each is coerced to 0 here rather than leaking None into a
 14196	        count that a caller will format or add up."""
 14197	        row = _exec(self,
 14198	            "SELECT COUNT(*) AS total, "
 14199	            "SUM(CASE WHEN decision='inject' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS injected, "
 14200	            "SUM(CASE WHEN decision='silent' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS silent, "
 14201	            "SUM(CASE WHEN judged='useful' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS useful, "
 14202	            "SUM(CASE WHEN judged='noise' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS noise, "
 14203	            "SUM(CASE WHEN judged='unjudged' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS unjudged "
 14204	            "FROM sentinel_interventions WHERE project_id=?",
 14205	            (project_id,)).fetchone()
 14206	        useful = int(row["useful"] or 0)
 14207	        noise = int(row["noise"] or 0)
 14208	        judged_total = useful + noise
 14209	        return {
 14210	            "total": int(row["total"] or 0),
 14211	            "injected": int(row["injected"] or 0),
 14212	            "silent": int(row["silent"] or 0),
 14213	            "useful": useful,
 14214	            "noise": noise,
 14215	            "unjudged": int(row["unjudged"] or 0),
 14216	            "useful_ratio": (float(useful) / judged_total
 14217	                             if judged_total else None),
 14218	        }
 14219	
 14220	    def recent_interventions(self, project_id, limit):
 14221	        """The most recent `limit` ledger rows for `project_id`, newest
 14222	        first, as plain dicts.
 14223	
 14224	        created_at DESC then rowid DESC for the same reason latest_status
 14225	        orders that way: timestamps here are second-precision, and the
 14226	        cooldown rule in the selection policy depends on 'the last N
 14227	        interventions' meaning the last N in the order they happened, not
 14228	        whichever order sqlite returned for a tied second."""
 14229	        rows = _exec(self,
 14230	            "SELECT * FROM sentinel_interventions WHERE project_id=? "
 14231	            "ORDER BY created_at DESC, rowid DESC LIMIT ?",
 14232	            (project_id, limit)).fetchall()
 14233	        return [dict(r) for r in rows]
 14234	
 14235	    def sentinel_orphans(self):
 14236	        """Read-only report: for each of the four Memory Sentinel tables,
 14237	        every project_id that has rows there but no row in `projects`, with
 14238	        a row count for each.
 14239	
 14240	        This is the gap purge_project's own SBE10 fix (this module's
 14241	        _require_sentinel_project docstring above) could not close: that
 14242	        fix stops NEW orphans and cleans a project's sentinel rows when
 14243	        THAT project is purged, but a sentinel row whose project_id never
 14244	        resolves has no project row for purge_project to key off, so
 14245	        purge_project refuses it ('not-found') and the row is unreachable
 14246	        by any erasure command that ships today. This report is how a
 14247	        caller FINDS those rows before purge_sentinel_orphans (below)
 14248	        erases them.
 14249	
 14250	        NEVER reads or returns a prose column (content, attempt,
 14251	        diagnosis, summary, open_risks, trigger, decision, reminder,
 14252	        reason): only project_id and COUNT(*), because this is a privacy
 14253	        tool and printing the sentinel prose to a terminal, a log, or a
 14254	        session transcript to report on it would be the exact leak this
 14255	        whole guard exists to prevent.
 14256	
 14257	        Returns {table_name: {project_id: row_count}}. A table with no
 14258	        orphan rows maps to {}."""
 14259	        result = {}
 14260	        for table in _TABLES_SENTINEL:
 14261	            rows = _exec(self,
 14262	                "SELECT project_id, COUNT(*) AS n FROM %s WHERE project_id "
 14263	                "NOT IN (SELECT project_id FROM projects) "
 14264	                "GROUP BY project_id" % table).fetchall()
 14265	            result[table] = {r["project_id"]: r["n"] for r in rows}
 14266	        return result
 14267	
 14268	    _SENTINEL_ORPHANS_ALL_TOKEN = "ALL-ORPHANS"
 14269	
 14270	    def purge_sentinel_orphans(self, actor, confirmation_token,
 14271	                                project_id=None, all_orphans=False,
 14272	                                dry_run=False):
 14273	        """Erase sentinel rows whose project_id resolves to no project,
 14274	        the rows sentinel_orphans() above finds and purge_project cannot
 14275	        reach. Guarded the way purge_project guards itself, because this
 14276	        is the same kind of irreversible deletion of raw founder prose:
 14277	
 14278	        Exactly one of `project_id` or `all_orphans=True` must be given
 14279	        (OwnershipRefused 'no-target' for neither, 'ambiguous-target' for
 14280	        both), so a caller always names a single, explicit target rather
 14281	        than defaulting into 'everything'.
 14282	
 14283	        `project_id` names ONE orphan project id to erase. Refused
 14284	        ('not-orphan') if a project row with that id still exists: this
 14285	        command only ever touches rows purge_project cannot reach, and a
 14286	        live project's sentinel rows are purge_project's job. Refused
 14287	        ('not-found') if no sentinel row anywhere names that id: a typo
 14288	        gets a refusal, not a silent no-op.
 14289	
 14290	        `all_orphans=True` erases every orphan row in all four tables,
 14291	        whichever project ids they name. Confirming this on purpose needs
 14292	        a literal token distinct from any real project id, so a caller
 14293	        cannot confirm 'all orphans' by accidentally typing a project id
 14294	        that happens to match nothing: confirmation_token must equal the
 14295	        literal string 'ALL-ORPHANS'.
 14296	
 14297	        Confirming a single `project_id` target follows purge_project's
 14298	        own rule exactly: confirmation_token must equal project_id.
 14299	        Neither token is required under dry_run, same reasoning as
 14300	        purge_project's own dry_run: the token stands between a caller
 14301	        and an irreversible deletion, and a dry run deletes nothing.
 14302	
 14303	        dry_run=True runs the real DELETE statements and rolls the whole
 14304	        transaction back (purge_project's own technique), so the counts
 14305	        returned are the real erasure's own rather than a second
 14306	        implementation's guess.
 14307	
 14308	        One attribution row ('sentinel.orphans_purged') is written per
 14309	        project id actually purged, inside the same transaction, mirroring
 14310	        purge_project's one-row-per-project shape; attribution carries no
 14311	        foreign key to projects (by design, see _write_attribution), so a
 14312	        row naming a project id that no longer exists, or never existed,
 14313	        is exactly what this method's own record looks like.
 14314	
 14315	        Returns {'removed': {project_id: {table: count, ...}, ...},
 14316	        'project_ids': [...]}."""
 14317	        if project_id and all_orphans:
 14318	            raise OwnershipRefused(
 14319	                "ambiguous-target",
 14320	                "name --project-id OR --all-orphans, not both; nothing "

  7054	    def fts_available(self):
  7055	        """Is the fast path in force RIGHT NOW for this open store?
  7056	
  7057	        Three conditions, all required: the founder asked for it, this SQLite
  7058	        can build one, and the index table exists. Anything else is False and
  7059	        retrieval stays lexical."""

  7062	    def _fts_exec(self, sql, params=()):
  7063	        """The ONE routing point for statements against the INDEX table.
  7064	
  7065	        Deliberately not _exec, and this is the whole point (P7 fix round).
  7066	        _exec sends any non-transient OperationalError to
  7067	        _quarantine_and_raise, which MOVES store.sqlite3 and its sidecars into
  7068	        a quarantine directory and raises StoreCorrupt. "no such table:
  7069	        learning_rule_fts" is exactly that shape, so an OPTIONAL accelerator
  7070	        losing its table mid-session took the founder's whole database with
  7071	        it, along with the approval in flight. Worse, the `except
  7072	        sqlite3.Error` guards written around those calls to keep the failure
  7073	        direction lexical could never fire: StoreCorrupt is not a subclass of
  7074	        sqlite3.Error, so they were dead code. Reproduced before this fix on a
  7075	        real store: drop the index table, approve a candidate, and the result
  7076	        was StoreCorrupt with the database gone.
  7077	
  7078	        So the index gets its own routing point with no quarantine in it. A
  7079	        sqlite error here comes back to the caller AS a sqlite3.Error, the
  7080	        caller switches the fast path off, and retrieval is lexical, which is
  7081	        the mode that always works. Statements against the REAL tables stay on
  7082	        _exec, where damage still means damage and quarantine is correct."""
  7083	        return self.conn.execute(sql, params)

 17048	    def dump(self, raw=False):
 17049	        """Full JSON-serializable export of every table.
 17050	
 17051	        GATE C: redacts BY DEFAULT-DENY. Every TEXT-typed column not in
 17052	        _DUMP_SAFE_COLUMNS (read live from the schema, see _text_columns)
 17053	        is redacted, whatever its name; an earlier enumerated allowlist of
 17054	        "known-sensitive" fields missed transitions.note, directives.text,
 17055	        records.evidence/check_cmd/owner precisely because nobody had
 17056	        listed them. dump is exactly what a founder pipes into a file, a
 17057	        paste, or an issue, so silent cleartext by default is the wrong
 17058	        direction.
 17059	
 17060	        LOOP 11: redaction was never enough on its own. redact_text is a
 17061	        secret SCRUBBER, and founder prose has no secret shape, so an
 17062	        ordinary dump still reproduced objectives, evidence, digest bodies,
 17063	        transition notes, decisions and directives verbatim. Every text
 17064	        column now goes through export_column, which WITHHOLDS by default
 17065	        and scrubs only records.name and records.tier. raw=True (CLI: --raw)
 17066	        is the explicit, named escape
 17067	        hatch for a human who really needs the unredacted sqlite contents
 17068	        (SECURITY.md documents the file itself as sensitive); never the
 17069	        default."""
 17070	        out = {}
 17071	        for t in _TABLES:
 17072	            rows = _exec(self, "SELECT * FROM %s" % t).fetchall()
 17073	            out[t] = [dict(r) for r in rows]
 17074	        if raw:
 17075	            return out
 17076	        for t in _TABLES:
 17077	            text_cols = _text_columns(self.conn, t)
 17078	            # A digest-shaped column is routed through the policy even when the
 17079	            # structural allowlist names it, because the shape rule outranks the
 17080	            # allowlist inside export_column and skipping it here would put that
 17081	            # decision back in two places.
 17082	            policy_cols = [c for c in text_cols
 17083	                           if (t, c) not in _DUMP_SAFE_COLUMNS
 17084	                           or c.endswith(_DUMP_DIGEST_SUFFIXES)]
 17085	            if not policy_cols:
 17086	                continue
 17087	            for row_dict in out[t]:
 17088	                for col in policy_cols:
 17089	                    if not row_dict.get(col):
 17090	                        continue
 17091	                    # ONE policy, shared with every other export: see
 17092	                    # export_column. Withheld by default, scrubbed only for
 17093	                    # the two identifier-shaped columns, and digests withheld
 17094	                    # by name-shape whatever list they appear on.
 17095	                    row_dict[col] = export_column(t, col, row_dict[col])
 17096	        return out

 17099	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 17100	# Read-only access (fix-round 4, 2026-07-26): verify, dump, and dashboard
 17101	# are diagnostics. A diagnostic that can write is a diagnostic that can
 17102	# silently CREATE the very thing it claims to be checking, and then report
 17103	# health about the empty shell it just made. This class never creates a
 17104	# directory, a file, or a WAL sidecar, never runs schema DDL, and opens the
 17105	# database file itself READ-ONLY, with PRAGMA query_only=ON kept as a
 17106	# second, independent defence (cross-family refuter finding 4, which
 17107	# reopened GATE A of fix-round 6; see _connect_read_only for what changed
 17108	# and what did not).
 17109	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 17110	
 17111	def _read_only_uri(path, query):
 17112	    """`path` as a sqlite file: URI carrying `query`, with the WHOLE path
 17113	    percent-encoded by pathlib rather than by hand.
 17114	
 17115	    GATE A (fix-round 6) deleted URIs from this file because the URI in use
 17116	    then escaped only '?' and '#' while sqlite percent-DECODES the rest of
 17117	    the filename, so a project at p%41 silently resolved to pA and every
 17118	    read-only command reported another project's database as this one's.
 17119	    That reasoning was about a PARTIAL escape, and the conclusion drawn
 17120	    from it, "escaping '%' too would still leave a pattern-language bug
 17121	    waiting for the next special character", is answered by not escaping
 17122	    characters one at a time: Path.as_uri() percent-encodes every byte
 17123	    outside the unreserved set, which is a total rule rather than a list.
 17124	    Verified on this machine (Python 3.9.6, sqlite 3.51.0, darwin) against
 17125	    pA, p%41, p[1], p#q, p?x, p a, p'q, pe, p+q, p&q, p=q and p%2Fq: each
 17126	    one opened its OWN database, and test_a_path_full_of_uri_
 17127	    metacharacters_still_opens_its_own_database is that property as a test
 17128	    rather than as this paragraph.
 17129	
 17130	    A URI is unavoidable here: mode=ro is the only way sqlite3 will open
 17131	    the database file itself read-only, and sqlite3.connect exposes it
 17132	    through the URI form alone."""
 17133	    if not os.path.isabs(path):
 17134	        path = os.path.abspath(path)
 17135	    return pathlib.Path(path).as_uri() + query
