 17137	
 17138	def _connect_read_only(path, timeout=5.0):
 17139	    """Open an EXISTING file GENUINELY read-only.
 17140	
 17141	    WHAT WAS WRONG (cross-family refuter, finding 4). This opened an
 17142	    ordinary read-WRITE sqlite3.connect(path) and only afterwards executed
 17143	    PRAGMA query_only=ON. query_only stops SQL statements from writing; it
 17144	    does not stop the OPEN itself from writing, and for a WAL database the
 17145	    open is a write: measured on this machine, a plain connect to a
 17146	    cleanly-closed WAL store whose sidecars had been removed CREATED both
 17147	    store.sqlite3-wal and store.sqlite3-shm and left them behind, so a
 17148	    purely diagnostic `bm-controller status` mutated the store directory.
 17149	    Where the directory forbids that, it was worse than a mutation: the
 17150	    open raised 'attempt to write a readonly database', which is not a
 17151	    transient-busy error, so ReadOnlyStore.__init__ quarantine-classified
 17152	    it and reported a PERFECTLY HEALTHY store as StoreCorrupt.
 17153	
 17154	    THE LADDER, and why it is a ladder rather than one spelling. sqlite has
 17155	    two read-only modes and neither covers both cases alone:
 17156	
 17157	      mode=ro           opens the database file O_RDONLY, reads THROUGH the
 17158	                        WAL with real locks, and is therefore the honest
 17159	                        answer whenever a WAL exists. It still needs the
 17160	                        -shm (sqlite's shared-memory bookkeeping, not the
 17161	                        database), and it cannot CREATE the -wal, so
 17162	                        against a store with no -wal at all it fails with
 17163	                        'unable to open database file'.
 17164	
 17165	      mode=ro&immutable=1
 17166	                        creates nothing whatsoever and works in a
 17167	                        directory with no write permission, because it
 17168	                        tells sqlite the file is not changing and so no
 17169	                        WAL, no -shm and no locking are needed.
 17170	
 17171	    So: with a -wal present, mode=ro, always, and never immutable, because
 17172	    immutable IGNORES the WAL and a diagnostic that silently reports the
 17173	    pre-WAL state of a live store is the same class of lie fix-round 4
 17174	    exists to prevent (test_a_pending_wal_is_read_through_and_not_ignored
 17175	    pins that). With NO -wal there is nothing pending by definition, and
 17176	    immutable is both the accurate description of the file and the only
 17177	    open that touches nothing.
 17178	
 17179	    THE RACE THIS LEAVES, stated rather than buried. A writer can create a
 17180	    -wal between the stat and the open, which would leave an immutable
 17181	    connection reading a file that is being checkpointed underneath it. The
 17182	    -wal is re-checked immediately after the open and the connection is
 17183	    thrown away and retaken WAL-aware if one appeared, so the residual
 17184	    window is a writer that opens, commits, checkpoints AND closes inside
 17185	    it. That is not closed, and cannot be from this side without taking the
 17186	    write lock a read-only diagnostic must not take.
 17187	
 17188	    PRAGMA query_only=ON is kept, as a second defence that does not share a
 17189	    failure mode with the first: mode=ro is enforced by the OS on the file
 17190	    handle, query_only by sqlite on the statement.
 17191	
 17192	    The whole ladder lives in THIS function, including both PRAGMA calls,
 17193	    rather than in an opening helper beside it. That is deliberate: GATE 4
 17194	    (test_structural_gate4_bare_execute_sites_are_all_named_exceptions)
 17195	    holds every bare .execute() site to a closed, named set, and
 17196	    _connect_read_only is already in it as "the one place a read-only
 17197	    connection is opened, before self.conn exists". Splitting the open out
 17198	    would have meant widening that set, and one exempt site is a smaller
 17199	    surface than two."""

 17201	    attempts = ["?mode=ro"]

 17203	        # No pending log, so nothing can be missed by not reading one, and
 17204	        # this is the only spelling that creates no sidecar at all. The
 17205	        # WAL-aware open stays behind it as the retry for the race the
 17206	        # docstring names.
 17207	        attempts.insert(0, "?mode=ro&immutable=1")
 17208	    conn = None
 17209	    for index, query in enumerate(attempts):
 17210	        try:
 17211	            conn = sqlite3.connect(_read_only_uri(path, query), uri=True,
 17212	                                   timeout=timeout, isolation_level=None)
 17213	            conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
 17214	            conn.execute("PRAGMA query_only = ON")
 17215	            conn.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000")
 17216	        except sqlite3.OperationalError as e:
 17217	            if conn is not None:
 17218	                try:
 17219	                    conn.close()
 17220	                except Exception:
 17221	                    pass
 17222	            if _is_transient_busy_error(e):
 17223	                raise
 17224	            raise _read_only_refusal(path, e)
 17225	        except Exception:
 17226	            if conn is not None:
 17227	                try:
 17228	                    conn.close()
 17229	                except Exception:
 17230	                    pass
 17231	            raise
 17232	        if index == len(attempts) - 1 or not os.path.exists(wal_path):
 17233	            return conn

 17235	        # the immutable view is no longer the whole truth. Throw it away
 17236	        # and take the WAL-aware one.
 17237	        try:
 17238	            conn.close()
 17239	        except Exception:
 17240	            pass

 17245	def _read_only_refusal(path, cause):
 17246	    """'store-unreadable', never StoreCorrupt: the file may be in perfect
 17247	    health and simply live somewhere a read-only connection cannot keep its
 17248	    WAL bookkeeping. Saying "corrupt" about that is a false diagnosis of
 17249	    the founder's data, which is the whole reason ReadOnlyStore stopped
 17250	    being allowed to quarantine anything."""
 17251	    return OwnershipRefused(
 17252	        "store-unreadable",
 17253	        "the store at %s could not be opened read-only (%s). The file "
 17254	        "itself is not implicated and nothing was written, moved or "
 17255	        "renamed: a WAL-mode database needs its %s sidecar, and a "
 17256	        "read-only connection can neither create one nor use the write-"
 17257	        "ahead log without it. Either make the directory writable for one "
 17258	        "run of a writable command (which checkpoints the log away), or "
 17259	        "copy the store, its -wal and its -shm together to somewhere "
 17260	        "writable and inspect it there."
 17261	        % (path, cause, os.path.basename(path) + "-shm"),
 17262	        details={"path": path, "cause": str(cause)})
 17263

 17265	class ReadOnlyStore(object):
 17266	    """Opens an EXISTING store read-only. Refuses 'no-store' before ever
 17267	    calling sqlite3.connect if the file is not already there (fix-round 4):
 17268	    the old behavior let verify/dump/dashboard silently create an empty
 17269	    store and then report it healthy, seconds after a truncation had just
 17270	    quarantined the real one. A zero-length existing file is corruption
 17271	    here exactly as it is for the writable Store (GATE B, fix-round 3)."""
 17272	
 17273	    def __init__(self, root):
 17274	        self.root = os.path.realpath(root)
 17275	        self.conn = None
 17276	        # GATE D (round 3) plus GATE B (round 5, 2026-07-26): the same
 17277	        # containment checks the writable Store applies, so a diagnostic
 17278	        # cannot be tricked by a symlinked .brothermode directory or a
 17279	        # symlinked store file/sidecar either.
 17280	        _refuse_if_symlink_escape(store_dir(self.root))
 17281	        # FINDING 5 (2026-07-27): the diagnostics run through this class, and
 17282	        # a diagnostic that reports a healthy store while git is committing
 17283	        # its cleartext contents is the same failure shape fix-round 4
 17284	        # already closed once ("report health about the empty shell it just
 17285	        # made"). Refusing is not writing: nothing here moves, renames or
 17286	        # creates anything, and the refusal message IS the diagnosis, naming
 17287	        # both the remediation and the escape hatch.
 17288	        _refuse_if_git_can_commit_store(self.root)
 17289	        self.path = store_path(self.root)
 17290	        if not os.path.isfile(self.path):
 17291	            raise OwnershipRefused(
 17292	                "no-store",
 17293	                "no store exists at %s; run `%s init` to create one"
 17294	                % (self.path, _cmd()),
 17295	                details={"path": self.path})
 17296	        _refuse_if_symlink_escape(self.path)
 17297	        _refuse_if_symlink_escape(self.path + "-wal")
 17298	        _refuse_if_symlink_escape(self.path + "-shm")
 17299	        # SOFT D (fix-round 7, 2026-07-26): same containment intent as the
 17300	        # symlink checks above, for the threat a symlink check cannot see.
 17301	        _refuse_if_hardlinked(self.path)
 17302	        _refuse_if_hardlinked(self.path + "-wal")
 17303	        _refuse_if_hardlinked(self.path + "-shm")
 17304	        if os.path.getsize(self.path) == 0:
 17305	            self._quarantine_and_raise(
 17306	                ValueError("store file exists but is zero bytes (truncated, or never finished writing)"))
 17307	        try:
 17308	            conn = _connect_read_only(self.path)
 17309	            self.conn = conn
 17310	            _OPEN_STORES.add(self)
 17311	            if _TRACK_UNCLOSED:
 17312	                _UNCLOSED.add(self)
 17313	            # CRITICAL A (fix-round 8): the same structural verification the
 17314	            # writable Store applies on an existing file, so a diagnostic
 17315	            # command (dashboard, dump, verify) run against a store missing
 17316	            # a table quarantines and says so, rather than opening "clean"
 17317	            # and reporting whatever is left as healthy.
 17318	            self._verify_schema_or_raise()
 17319	            self.conn.execute("SELECT value FROM meta WHERE key='schema_version'").fetchone()
 17320	        except sqlite3.OperationalError as e:
 17321	            if not _is_transient_busy_error(e):
 17322	                self._quarantine_and_raise(e)
 17323	            if self.conn is not None:
 17324	                try:
 17325	                    self.conn.close()
 17326	                except Exception:
 17327	                    pass
 17328	                self.conn = None
 17329	                _UNCLOSED.discard(self)
 17330	            raise OwnershipRefused(
 17331	                "db-busy",
 17332	                "the store at %s is busy or locked (%s); wait a moment and "
 17333	                "retry." % (self.path, e))
 17334	        except sqlite3.DatabaseError as e:
 17335	            self._quarantine_and_raise(e)
 17336	
 17337	    def _quarantine_and_raise(self, cause):
 17338	        """FINDING 7, CONFIRMED: a read-only health check must NEVER move the
 17339	        real database. This used to be one line, `return
 17340	        Store._quarantine_and_raise(self, cause)`, so every quarantine the
 17341	        writable store could perform, a diagnostic could perform too. The
 17342	        SessionStart hook runs `bm_store.py verify` automatically against
 17343	        the founder's real project, so a transient disk I/O error, a
 17344	        permission problem or a network-volume hiccup during that read was
 17345	        enough to move a healthy store aside.
 17346	
 17347	        Overriding here rather than checking a flag inside the mover is the
 17348	        point: ReadOnlyStore does not inherit from Store, so with this
 17349	        delegation removed there is NO path from a read-only object to the
 17350	        code that renames or moves anything. Write authority, not error
 17351	        classification, decides whether the destructive action is reachable
 17352	        at all.
 17353	
 17354	        Reports and preserves: the connection is closed (so the handle
 17355	        cannot outlive the failure, the leak class from CI run 18) and the
 17356	        file is left byte for byte where it was. StoreCorrupt is
 17357	        deliberately the same exception type this raised before, so every
 17358	        caller outside this module keeps the behavior it already handles;
 17359	        only the side effect is gone."""
 17360	        self.close()
 17361	        raise StoreCorrupt(
 17362	            "%s could not be read as a SQLite database (%s). This was a "
 17363	            "READ-ONLY diagnostic, which has no authority to write: nothing "
 17364	            "was moved, renamed, copied or deleted, and the file is still at "
 17365	            "its original path with its original bytes. Inspect it by hand; "
 17366	            "if it really is damaged, a writable command is the only thing "
 17367	            "that may quarantine it." % (self.path, cause))
 17368	
 17369	    def _verify_schema_or_raise(self):
 17370	        # Reuses Store's implementation verbatim, same reasoning as above.
 17371	        # migrate is left at its default False: a read-only diagnostic must
 17372	        # never migrate. It gets a clear "needs migration" refusal instead.
 17373	        return Store._verify_schema_or_raise(self)
 17374	
 17375	    def _refuse_without_quarantine(self, message, reason=None):
 17376	        """Borrowed for the same reason as _verify_schema_or_raise above, and
 17377	        MISSING until a live probe caught it (correction-learning Loop 1,
 17378	        2026-07-29): opening a real schema-1 store with a schema-2 binary
 17379	        through `verify` raised AttributeError instead of the intended refusal.
 17380	        All 419 tests were green, because not one of them opened an
 17381	        out-of-date store through the READ-ONLY path. The regression test for
 17382	        this is test_readonly_store_on_schema1_refuses_cleanly.
 17383	
 17384	        `reason` is passed straight through (2026-08-04), because this class is
 17385	        where the schema-behind refusal actually reaches a founder: verify,
 17386	        dump and dashboard all read through here, and they are what printed the
 17387	        false "STORE CORRUPT". The writable Store keeps migrate=True on every
 17388	        open, so it only reaches that branch through _migrate_from's own
 17389	        post-migration re-verify, which cannot see an unmoved version unless a
 17390	        migration is broken."""
 17391	        return Store._refuse_without_quarantine(self, message, reason=reason)
 17392	
 17393	    def dump(self, raw=False):
 17394	        # Reuses Store.dump() verbatim: it only calls _exec(self, ...) over
 17395	        # _TABLES, which works identically against a read-only connection.
 17396	        return Store.dump(self, raw=raw)

 17400	        # read-only consumer resolving a name needs this at least as much

 17100	# Read-only access (fix-round 4, 2026-07-26): verify, dump, and dashboard
 17101	# are diagnostics. A diagnostic that can write is a diagnostic that can
 17102	# silently CREATE the very thing it claims to be checking, and then report
 17103	# health about the empty shell it just made. This class never creates a
 17104	# directory, a file, or a WAL sidecar, never runs schema DDL, and opens the
 17105	# database file itself READ-ONLY, with PRAGMA query_only=ON kept as a
 17106	# second, independent defence (cross-family refuter finding 4, which
 17107	# reopened GATE A of fix-round 6; see _connect_read_only for what changed
 17108	# and what did not).
 17109	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 17110	
 17111	def _read_only_uri(path, query):
 17112	    """`path` as a sqlite file: URI carrying `query`, with the WHOLE path
 17113	    percent-encoded by pathlib rather than by hand.
 17114	
 17115	    GATE A (fix-round 6) deleted URIs from this file because the URI in use
 17116	    then escaped only '?' and '#' while sqlite percent-DECODES the rest of
 17117	    the filename, so a project at p%41 silently resolved to pA and every
 17118	    read-only command reported another project's database as this one's.
 17119	    That reasoning was about a PARTIAL escape, and the conclusion drawn
 17120	    from it, "escaping '%' too would still leave a pattern-language bug
 17121	    waiting for the next special character", is answered by not escaping
 17122	    characters one at a time: Path.as_uri() percent-encodes every byte
 17123	    outside the unreserved set, which is a total rule rather than a list.
 17124	    Verified on this machine (Python 3.9.6, sqlite 3.51.0, darwin) against
 17125	    pA, p%41, p[1], p#q, p?x, p a, p'q, pe, p+q, p&q, p=q and p%2Fq: each
 17126	    one opened its OWN database, and test_a_path_full_of_uri_
 17127	    metacharacters_still_opens_its_own_database is that property as a test
 17128	    rather than as this paragraph.
 17129	
 17130	    A URI is unavoidable here: mode=ro is the only way sqlite3 will open
 17131	    the database file itself read-only, and sqlite3.connect exposes it
 17132	    through the URI form alone."""
 17133	    if not os.path.isabs(path):
 17134	        path = os.path.abspath(path)
 17135	    return pathlib.Path(path).as_uri() + query

 17138	def _connect_read_only(path, timeout=5.0):
 17139	    """Open an EXISTING file GENUINELY read-only.
 17140	
 17141	    WHAT WAS WRONG (cross-family refuter, finding 4). This opened an
 17142	    ordinary read-WRITE sqlite3.connect(path) and only afterwards executed
 17143	    PRAGMA query_only=ON. query_only stops SQL statements from writing; it
 17144	    does not stop the OPEN itself from writing, and for a WAL database the
 17145	    open is a write: measured on this machine, a plain connect to a
 17146	    cleanly-closed WAL store whose sidecars had been removed CREATED both
 17147	    store.sqlite3-wal and store.sqlite3-shm and left them behind, so a
 17148	    purely diagnostic `bm-controller status` mutated the store directory.
 17149	    Where the directory forbids that, it was worse than a mutation: the
 17150	    open raised 'attempt to write a readonly database', which is not a
 17151	    transient-busy error, so ReadOnlyStore.__init__ quarantine-classified
 17152	    it and reported a PERFECTLY HEALTHY store as StoreCorrupt.
 17153	
 17154	    THE LADDER, and why it is a ladder rather than one spelling. sqlite has
 17155	    two read-only modes and neither covers both cases alone:
 17156	
 17157	      mode=ro           opens the database file O_RDONLY, reads THROUGH the
 17158	                        WAL with real locks, and is therefore the honest
 17159	                        answer whenever a WAL exists. It still needs the
 17160	                        -shm (sqlite's shared-memory bookkeeping, not the
 17161	                        database), and it cannot CREATE the -wal, so
 17162	                        against a store with no -wal at all it fails with
 17163	                        'unable to open database file'.
 17164	
 17165	      mode=ro&immutable=1
 17166	                        creates nothing whatsoever and works in a
 17167	                        directory with no write permission, because it
 17168	                        tells sqlite the file is not changing and so no
 17169	                        WAL, no -shm and no locking are needed.
 17170	
 17171	    So: with a -wal present, mode=ro, always, and never immutable, because
 17172	    immutable IGNORES the WAL and a diagnostic that silently reports the
 17173	    pre-WAL state of a live store is the same class of lie fix-round 4
 17174	    exists to prevent (test_a_pending_wal_is_read_through_and_not_ignored
 17175	    pins that). With NO -wal there is nothing pending by definition, and
 17176	    immutable is both the accurate description of the file and the only
 17177	    open that touches nothing.
 17178	
 17179	    THE RACE THIS LEAVES, stated rather than buried. A writer can create a
 17180	    -wal between the stat and the open, which would leave an immutable
 17181	    connection reading a file that is being checkpointed underneath it. The
 17182	    -wal is re-checked immediately after the open and the connection is
 17183	    thrown away and retaken WAL-aware if one appeared, so the residual
 17184	    window is a writer that opens, commits, checkpoints AND closes inside
 17185	    it. That is not closed, and cannot be from this side without taking the
 17186	    write lock a read-only diagnostic must not take.
 17187	
 17188	    PRAGMA query_only=ON is kept, as a second defence that does not share a
 17189	    failure mode with the first: mode=ro is enforced by the OS on the file
 17190	    handle, query_only by sqlite on the statement.
 17191	
 17192	    The whole ladder lives in THIS function, including both PRAGMA calls,
 17193	    rather than in an opening helper beside it. That is deliberate: GATE 4
 17194	    (test_structural_gate4_bare_execute_sites_are_all_named_exceptions)
 17195	    holds every bare .execute() site to a closed, named set, and
 17196	    _connect_read_only is already in it as "the one place a read-only
 17197	    connection is opened, before self.conn exists". Splitting the open out
 17198	    would have meant widening that set, and one exempt site is a smaller
 17199	    surface than two."""
 17200	    wal_path = path + "-wal"
 17201	    attempts = ["?mode=ro"]
 17202	    if not os.path.exists(wal_path):
 17203	        # No pending log, so nothing can be missed by not reading one, and
 17204	        # this is the only spelling that creates no sidecar at all. The
 17205	        # WAL-aware open stays behind it as the retry for the race the
 17206	        # docstring names.
 17207	        attempts.insert(0, "?mode=ro&immutable=1")
 17208	    conn = None
 17209	    for index, query in enumerate(attempts):
 17210	        try:
 17211	            conn = sqlite3.connect(_read_only_uri(path, query), uri=True,
 17212	                                   timeout=timeout, isolation_level=None)
 17213	            conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
 17214	            conn.execute("PRAGMA query_only = ON")
 17215	            conn.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000")
 17216	        except sqlite3.OperationalError as e:
 17217	            if conn is not None:
 17218	                try:
 17219	                    conn.close()
 17220	                except Exception:
 17221	                    pass
 17222	            if _is_transient_busy_error(e):
 17223	                raise
 17224	            raise _read_only_refusal(path, e)
 17225	        except Exception:
 17226	            if conn is not None:
 17227	                try:
 17228	                    conn.close()
 17229	                except Exception:
 17230	                    pass
 17231	            raise
 17232	        if index == len(attempts) - 1 or not os.path.exists(wal_path):
 17233	            return conn
 17234	        # A writer created a WAL between the stat above and this open, so
 17235	        # the immutable view is no longer the whole truth. Throw it away
 17236	        # and take the WAL-aware one.
 17237	        try:
 17238	            conn.close()
 17239	        except Exception:
 17240	            pass
 17241	        conn = None
 17242	    raise AssertionError("unreachable: the attempt ladder always returns")
 17243

 17245	def _read_only_refusal(path, cause):
 17246	    """'store-unreadable', never StoreCorrupt: the file may be in perfect
 17247	    health and simply live somewhere a read-only connection cannot keep its
 17248	    WAL bookkeeping. Saying "corrupt" about that is a false diagnosis of
 17249	    the founder's data, which is the whole reason ReadOnlyStore stopped
 17250	    being allowed to quarantine anything."""
 17251	    return OwnershipRefused(
 17252	        "store-unreadable",
 17253	        "the store at %s could not be opened read-only (%s). The file "
 17254	        "itself is not implicated and nothing was written, moved or "
 17255	        "renamed: a WAL-mode database needs its %s sidecar, and a "
 17256	        "read-only connection can neither create one nor use the write-"
 17257	        "ahead log without it. Either make the directory writable for one "
 17258	        "run of a writable command (which checkpoints the log away), or "
 17259	        "copy the store, its -wal and its -shm together to somewhere "
 17260	        "writable and inspect it there."
 17261	        % (path, cause, os.path.basename(path) + "-shm"),
 17262	        details={"path": path, "cause": str(cause)})

 17265	class ReadOnlyStore(object):
 17266	    """Opens an EXISTING store read-only. Refuses 'no-store' before ever
 17267	    calling sqlite3.connect if the file is not already there (fix-round 4):
 17268	    the old behavior let verify/dump/dashboard silently create an empty
 17269	    store and then report it healthy, seconds after a truncation had just
 17270	    quarantined the real one. A zero-length existing file is corruption
 17271	    here exactly as it is for the writable Store (GATE B, fix-round 3)."""
 17272	
 17273	    def __init__(self, root):
 17274	        self.root = os.path.realpath(root)
 17275	        self.conn = None
 17276	        # GATE D (round 3) plus GATE B (round 5, 2026-07-26): the same
 17277	        # containment checks the writable Store applies, so a diagnostic
 17278	        # cannot be tricked by a symlinked .brothermode directory or a
 17279	        # symlinked store file/sidecar either.
 17280	        _refuse_if_symlink_escape(store_dir(self.root))
 17281	        # FINDING 5 (2026-07-27): the diagnostics run through this class, and
 17282	        # a diagnostic that reports a healthy store while git is committing
 17283	        # its cleartext contents is the same failure shape fix-round 4
 17284	        # already closed once ("report health about the empty shell it just
 17285	        # made"). Refusing is not writing: nothing here moves, renames or
 17286	        # creates anything, and the refusal message IS the diagnosis, naming
 17287	        # both the remediation and the escape hatch.
 17288	        _refuse_if_git_can_commit_store(self.root)
 17289	        self.path = store_path(self.root)
 17290	        if not os.path.isfile(self.path):
 17291	            raise OwnershipRefused(
 17292	                "no-store",
 17293	                "no store exists at %s; run `%s init` to create one"
 17294	                % (self.path, _cmd()),
 17295	                details={"path": self.path})
 17296	        _refuse_if_symlink_escape(self.path)
 17297	        _refuse_if_symlink_escape(self.path + "-wal")
 17298	        _refuse_if_symlink_escape(self.path + "-shm")
 17299	        # SOFT D (fix-round 7, 2026-07-26): same containment intent as the
 17300	        # symlink checks above, for the threat a symlink check cannot see.
 17301	        _refuse_if_hardlinked(self.path)
 17302	        _refuse_if_hardlinked(self.path + "-wal")
 17303	        _refuse_if_hardlinked(self.path + "-shm")
 17304	        if os.path.getsize(self.path) == 0:
 17305	            self._quarantine_and_raise(
 17306	                ValueError("store file exists but is zero bytes (truncated, or never finished writing)"))
 17307	        try:
 17308	            conn = _connect_read_only(self.path)
 17309	            self.conn = conn
 17310	            _OPEN_STORES.add(self)
 17311	            if _TRACK_UNCLOSED:
 17312	                _UNCLOSED.add(self)
 17313	            # CRITICAL A (fix-round 8): the same structural verification the
 17314	            # writable Store applies on an existing file, so a diagnostic
 17315	            # command (dashboard, dump, verify) run against a store missing
 17316	            # a table quarantines and says so, rather than opening "clean"
 17317	            # and reporting whatever is left as healthy.
 17318	            self._verify_schema_or_raise()
 17319	            self.conn.execute("SELECT value FROM meta WHERE key='schema_version'").fetchone()
 17320	        except sqlite3.OperationalError as e:
 17321	            if not _is_transient_busy_error(e):
 17322	                self._quarantine_and_raise(e)
 17323	            if self.conn is not None:
 17324	                try:
 17325	                    self.conn.close()
 17326	                except Exception:
 17327	                    pass
 17328	                self.conn = None
 17329	                _UNCLOSED.discard(self)
 17330	            raise OwnershipRefused(
 17380	        All 419 tests were green, because not one of them opened an
 17381	        out-of-date store through the READ-ONLY path. The regression test for
 17382	        this is test_readonly_store_on_schema1_refuses_cleanly.
 17383	
 17384	        `reason` is passed straight through (2026-08-04), because this class is
 17385	        where the schema-behind refusal actually reaches a founder: verify,
 17386	        dump and dashboard all read through here, and they are what printed the

 17389	        post-migration re-verify, which cannot see an unmoved version unless a

 17111	def _read_only_uri(path, query):
 17112	    """`path` as a sqlite file: URI carrying `query`, with the WHOLE path
 17113	    percent-encoded by pathlib rather than by hand.
 17114	
 17115	    GATE A (fix-round 6) deleted URIs from this file because the URI in use
 17116	    then escaped only '?' and '#' while sqlite percent-DECODES the rest of
 17117	    the filename, so a project at p%41 silently resolved to pA and every
 17118	    read-only command reported another project's database as this one's.
 17119	    That reasoning was about a PARTIAL escape, and the conclusion drawn
 17120	    from it, "escaping '%' too would still leave a pattern-language bug
 17121	    waiting for the next special character", is answered by not escaping
 17122	    characters one at a time: Path.as_uri() percent-encodes every byte
 17123	    outside the unreserved set, which is a total rule rather than a list.
 17124	    Verified on this machine (Python 3.9.6, sqlite 3.51.0, darwin) against
 17125	    pA, p%41, p[1], p#q, p?x, p a, p'q, pe, p+q, p&q, p=q and p%2Fq: each
 17126	    one opened its OWN database, and test_a_path_full_of_uri_
 17127	    metacharacters_still_opens_its_own_database is that property as a test
 17128	    rather than as this paragraph.
 17129	
 17130	    A URI is unavoidable here: mode=ro is the only way sqlite3 will open

 17138	def _connect_read_only(path, timeout=5.0):
 17139	    """Open an EXISTING file GENUINELY read-only.
 17140	
 17141	    WHAT WAS WRONG (cross-family refuter, finding 4). This opened an
 17142	    ordinary read-WRITE sqlite3.connect(path) and only afterwards executed
 17143	    PRAGMA query_only=ON. query_only stops SQL statements from writing; it
 17144	    does not stop the OPEN itself from writing, and for a WAL database the
 17145	    open is a write: measured on this machine, a plain connect to a
 17146	    cleanly-closed WAL store whose sidecars had been removed CREATED both
 17147	    store.sqlite3-wal and store.sqlite3-shm and left them behind, so a
 17148	    purely diagnostic `bm-controller status` mutated the store directory.
 17149	    Where the directory forbids that, it was worse than a mutation: the
 17150	    open raised 'attempt to write a readonly database', which is not a
 17151	    transient-busy error, so ReadOnlyStore.__init__ quarantine-classified
 17152	    it and reported a PERFECTLY HEALTHY store as StoreCorrupt.
 17153	
 17154	    THE LADDER, and why it is a ladder rather than one spelling. sqlite has
 17155	    two read-only modes and neither covers both cases alone:
 17156	
 17157	      mode=ro           opens the database file O_RDONLY, reads THROUGH the
 17158	                        WAL with real locks, and is therefore the honest
 17159	                        answer whenever a WAL exists. It still needs the
 17160	                        -shm (sqlite's shared-memory bookkeeping, not the
 17161	                        database), and it cannot CREATE the -wal, so
 17162	                        against a store with no -wal at all it fails with
 17163	                        'unable to open database file'.
 17164	
 17165	      mode=ro&immutable=1
 17166	                        creates nothing whatsoever and works in a
 17167	                        directory with no write permission, because it
 17168	                        tells sqlite the file is not changing and so no
 17169	                        WAL, no -shm and no locking are needed.
 17170	
 17171	    So: with a -wal present, mode=ro, always, and never immutable, because
 17172	    immutable IGNORES the WAL and a diagnostic that silently reports the
 17173	    pre-WAL state of a live store is the same class of lie fix-round 4
 17174	    exists to prevent (test_a_pending_wal_is_read_through_and_not_ignored
 17175	    pins that). With NO -wal there is nothing pending by definition, and
 17176	    immutable is both the accurate description of the file and the only
 17177	    open that touches nothing.
 17178	
 17179	    THE RACE THIS LEAVES, stated rather than buried. A writer can create a
 17180	    -wal between the stat and the open, which would leave an immutable
 17181	    connection reading a file that is being checkpointed underneath it. The
 17182	    -wal is re-checked immediately after the open and the connection is
 17183	    thrown away and retaken WAL-aware if one appeared, so the residual
 17184	    window is a writer that opens, commits, checkpoints AND closes inside
 17185	    it. That is not closed, and cannot be from this side without taking the
 17186	    write lock a read-only diagnostic must not take.
 17187	
 17188	    PRAGMA query_only=ON is kept, as a second defence that does not share a
 17189	    failure mode with the first: mode=ro is enforced by the OS on the file
 17190	    handle, query_only by sqlite on the statement.
 17191	
 17192	    The whole ladder lives in THIS function, including both PRAGMA calls,
 17193	    rather than in an opening helper beside it. That is deliberate: GATE 4
 17194	    (test_structural_gate4_bare_execute_sites_are_all_named_exceptions)
 17195	    holds every bare .execute() site to a closed, named set, and
 17196	    _connect_read_only is already in it as "the one place a read-only
 17197	    connection is opened, before self.conn exists". Splitting the open out
 17198	    would have meant widening that set, and one exempt site is a smaller
 17199	    surface than two."""
 17200	    wal_path = path + "-wal"
 17201	    attempts = ["?mode=ro"]
 17202	    if not os.path.exists(wal_path):
 17203	        # No pending log, so nothing can be missed by not reading one, and
 17204	        # this is the only spelling that creates no sidecar at all. The
 17205	        # WAL-aware open stays behind it as the retry for the race the
 17206	        # docstring names.
 17207	        attempts.insert(0, "?mode=ro&immutable=1")
 17208	    conn = None
 17209	    for index, query in enumerate(attempts):
 17210	        try:
 17211	            conn = sqlite3.connect(_read_only_uri(path, query), uri=True,
 17212	                                   timeout=timeout, isolation_level=None)
 17213	            conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
 17214	            conn.execute("PRAGMA query_only = ON")
 17215	            conn.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000")
 17216	        except sqlite3.OperationalError as e:
 17217	            if conn is not None:
 17218	                try:
 17219	                    conn.close()
 17220	                except Exception:
 17221	                    pass
 17222	            if _is_transient_busy_error(e):
 17223	                raise
 17224	            raise _read_only_refusal(path, e)
 17225	        except Exception:
 17226	            if conn is not None:
 17227	                try:
 17228	                    conn.close()
 17229	                except Exception:
 17230	                    pass
 17231	            raise
 17232	        if index == len(attempts) - 1 or not os.path.exists(wal_path):
 17233	            return conn
 17234	        # A writer created a WAL between the stat above and this open, so
 17235	        # the immutable view is no longer the whole truth. Throw it away
 17236	        # and take the WAL-aware one.
 17237	        try:
 17238	            conn.close()
 17239	        except Exception:
 17240	            pass
 17241	        conn = None
 17242	    raise AssertionError("unreachable: the attempt ladder always returns")
 17243	
 17244	
 17245	def _read_only_refusal(path, cause):
 17246	    """'store-unreadable', never StoreCorrupt: the file may be in perfect
 17247	    health and simply live somewhere a read-only connection cannot keep its
 17248	    WAL bookkeeping. Saying "corrupt" about that is a false diagnosis of
 17249	    the founder's data, which is the whole reason ReadOnlyStore stopped
 17250	    being allowed to quarantine anything."""
 17251	    return OwnershipRefused(
 17252	        "store-unreadable",
 17253	        "the store at %s could not be opened read-only (%s). The file "
 17254	        "itself is not implicated and nothing was written, moved or "
 17255	        "renamed: a WAL-mode database needs its %s sidecar, and a "
 17256	        "read-only connection can neither create one nor use the write-"
 17257	        "ahead log without it. Either make the directory writable for one "
 17258	        "run of a writable command (which checkpoints the log away), or "
 17259	        "copy the store, its -wal and its -shm together to somewhere "
 17260	        "writable and inspect it there."
 17261	        % (path, cause, os.path.basename(path) + "-shm"),
 17262	        details={"path": path, "cause": str(cause)})
 17263	
 17264

 17266	    """Opens an EXISTING store read-only. Refuses 'no-store' before ever
 17267	    calling sqlite3.connect if the file is not already there (fix-round 4):
 17268	    the old behavior let verify/dump/dashboard silently create an empty
 17269	    store and then report it healthy, seconds after a truncation had just
 17270	    quarantined the real one. A zero-length existing file is corruption

 17265	class ReadOnlyStore(object):
 17266	    """Opens an EXISTING store read-only. Refuses 'no-store' before ever
 17267	    calling sqlite3.connect if the file is not already there (fix-round 4):
 17268	    the old behavior let verify/dump/dashboard silently create an empty
 17269	    store and then report it healthy, seconds after a truncation had just
 17270	    quarantined the real one. A zero-length existing file is corruption
 17271	    here exactly as it is for the writable Store (GATE B, fix-round 3)."""
 17272	
 17273	    def __init__(self, root):
 17274	        self.root = os.path.realpath(root)
 17275	        self.conn = None
 17276	        # GATE D (round 3) plus GATE B (round 5, 2026-07-26): the same
 17277	        # containment checks the writable Store applies, so a diagnostic
 17278	        # cannot be tricked by a symlinked .brothermode directory or a
 17279	        # symlinked store file/sidecar either.
 17280	        _refuse_if_symlink_escape(store_dir(self.root))
 17281	        # FINDING 5 (2026-07-27): the diagnostics run through this class, and
 17282	        # a diagnostic that reports a healthy store while git is committing
 17283	        # its cleartext contents is the same failure shape fix-round 4
 17284	        # already closed once ("report health about the empty shell it just
 17285	        # made"). Refusing is not writing: nothing here moves, renames or
 17286	        # creates anything, and the refusal message IS the diagnosis, naming
 17287	        # both the remediation and the escape hatch.
 17288	        _refuse_if_git_can_commit_store(self.root)
 17289	        self.path = store_path(self.root)
 17290	        if not os.path.isfile(self.path):
 17291	            raise OwnershipRefused(
 17292	                "no-store",
 17293	                "no store exists at %s; run `%s init` to create one"
 17294	                % (self.path, _cmd()),
 17295	                details={"path": self.path})
 17296	        _refuse_if_symlink_escape(self.path)
 17297	        _refuse_if_symlink_escape(self.path + "-wal")
 17298	        _refuse_if_symlink_escape(self.path + "-shm")
 17299	        # SOFT D (fix-round 7, 2026-07-26): same containment intent as the
 17300	        # symlink checks above, for the threat a symlink check cannot see.
 17301	        _refuse_if_hardlinked(self.path)
 17302	        _refuse_if_hardlinked(self.path + "-wal")
 17303	        _refuse_if_hardlinked(self.path + "-shm")
 17304	        if os.path.getsize(self.path) == 0:
 17305	            self._quarantine_and_raise(
 17306	                ValueError("store file exists but is zero bytes (truncated, or never finished writing)"))
 17307	        try:
 17308	            conn = _connect_read_only(self.path)
 17309	            self.conn = conn
 17310	            _OPEN_STORES.add(self)
 17311	            if _TRACK_UNCLOSED:
 17312	                _UNCLOSED.add(self)
 17313	            # CRITICAL A (fix-round 8): the same structural verification the
 17314	            # writable Store applies on an existing file, so a diagnostic
 17315	            # command (dashboard, dump, verify) run against a store missing
 17316	            # a table quarantines and says so, rather than opening "clean"
 17317	            # and reporting whatever is left as healthy.
 17318	            self._verify_schema_or_raise()
 17319	            self.conn.execute("SELECT value FROM meta WHERE key='schema_version'").fetchone()
 17320	        except sqlite3.OperationalError as e:
 17321	            if not _is_transient_busy_error(e):
 17322	                self._quarantine_and_raise(e)
 17323	            if self.conn is not None:
 17324	                try:
 17325	                    self.conn.close()
 17326	                except Exception:
 17327	                    pass
 17328	                self.conn = None
 17329	                _UNCLOSED.discard(self)
 17330	            raise OwnershipRefused(
 17331	                "db-busy",
 17332	                "the store at %s is busy or locked (%s); wait a moment and "
 17333	                "retry." % (self.path, e))
 17334	        except sqlite3.DatabaseError as e:
 17335	            self._quarantine_and_raise(e)
 17336	
 17337	    def _quarantine_and_raise(self, cause):
 17338	        """FINDING 7, CONFIRMED: a read-only health check must NEVER move the
 17339	        real database. This used to be one line, `return
 17340	        Store._quarantine_and_raise(self, cause)`, so every quarantine the
 17341	        writable store could perform, a diagnostic could perform too. The
 17342	        SessionStart hook runs `bm_store.py verify` automatically against
 17343	        the founder's real project, so a transient disk I/O error, a
 17344	        permission problem or a network-volume hiccup during that read was
 17345	        enough to move a healthy store aside.
 17346	
 17347	        Overriding here rather than checking a flag inside the mover is the
 17348	        point: ReadOnlyStore does not inherit from Store, so with this
 17349	        delegation removed there is NO path from a read-only object to the
 17350	        code that renames or moves anything. Write authority, not error
 17351	        classification, decides whether the destructive action is reachable
 17352	        at all.
 17353	
 17354	        Reports and preserves: the connection is closed (so the handle
 17355	        cannot outlive the failure, the leak class from CI run 18) and the
 17356	        file is left byte for byte where it was. StoreCorrupt is
 17357	        deliberately the same exception type this raised before, so every
 17358	        caller outside this module keeps the behavior it already handles;
 17359	        only the side effect is gone."""
 17360	        self.close()
 17361	        raise StoreCorrupt(
 17362	            "%s could not be read as a SQLite database (%s). This was a "
 17363	            "READ-ONLY diagnostic, which has no authority to write: nothing "
 17364	            "was moved, renamed, copied or deleted, and the file is still at "
 17365	            "its original path with its original bytes. Inspect it by hand; "
 17366	            "if it really is damaged, a writable command is the only thing "
 17367	            "that may quarantine it." % (self.path, cause))
 17368	
 17369	    def _verify_schema_or_raise(self):
 17370	        # Reuses Store's implementation verbatim, same reasoning as above.
 17371	        # migrate is left at its default False: a read-only diagnostic must
 17372	        # never migrate. It gets a clear "needs migration" refusal instead.
 17373	        return Store._verify_schema_or_raise(self)
 17374	
 17375	    def _refuse_without_quarantine(self, message, reason=None):
 17376	        """Borrowed for the same reason as _verify_schema_or_raise above, and
 17377	        MISSING until a live probe caught it (correction-learning Loop 1,
 17378	        2026-07-29): opening a real schema-1 store with a schema-2 binary
 17379	        through `verify` raised AttributeError instead of the intended refusal.
 17380	        All 419 tests were green, because not one of them opened an
 17381	        out-of-date store through the READ-ONLY path. The regression test for
 17382	        this is test_readonly_store_on_schema1_refuses_cleanly.
 17383	
 17384	        `reason` is passed straight through (2026-08-04), because this class is
 17385	        where the schema-behind refusal actually reaches a founder: verify,
 17386	        dump and dashboard all read through here, and they are what printed the
 17387	        false "STORE CORRUPT". The writable Store keeps migrate=True on every
 17388	        open, so it only reaches that branch through _migrate_from's own
 17389	        post-migration re-verify, which cannot see an unmoved version unless a
 17390	        migration is broken."""
 17391	        return Store._refuse_without_quarantine(self, message, reason=reason)
 17392	
 17393	    def dump(self, raw=False):
 17394	        # Reuses Store.dump() verbatim: it only calls _exec(self, ...) over
 17395	        # _TABLES, which works identically against a read-only connection.
 17396	        return Store.dump(self, raw=raw)

codex
The SQLite side is not snapshot-safe: each accessor is an independent autocommit read, so a writer can commit between project/tasks/attribution/decisions/evidence queries and produce a passport that never corresponded to any store state. I also found a separate validator/schema mismatch and am checking strict-JSON and cross-platform byte behavior before assigning severities.

     2	"""Regression tests for tools/bm_passport.py, the change passport's producer
     3	half (docs/specs/2026-08-15-change-passport-seam.md in the BrotherSBE
     4	repository is the contract this tool fills a hole in).
     5	
     6	WHAT THIS SUITE IS ACTUALLY DEFENDING
     7	  Three rules from the contract, each load bearing and each proven by a test
     8	  whose guard was removed by hand to watch it go red:
     9	
    10	  1. A hollow value is never written. An empty string, an empty list, or a
    11	     null reads as absence on the consuming side, so a field this tool
    12	     cannot establish honestly must be OMITTED, never padded to look
    13	     filled.
    14	  2. Direction of travel: this tool writes the deposit and reads NOTHING
    15	     under .sbe/. A poisoned tasks.json sitting next to a healthy store
    16	     must change nothing about what this tool produces.
    17	  3. Absent, corrupt, and healthy stores are three DIFFERENT reported
    18	     states, and none of the three ever crashes the tool.
    19	
    20	Every fixture is a tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(), never the real project
    21	store. The CLI is invoked as a real subprocess so its argument parsing,
    22	exit codes, and stdout are exercised exactly as a caller would see them.
    23	
    24	ADDED: the `generate` verb (schema/change-passport.v1.json), a second,
    25	additive CLI shape on the same tool that writes the full five field
    26	passport rather than the three field deposit above. Its own section below
    27	covers: a round trip from a fixture store built through the Store write
    28	API (never a hand written store file), determinism across two generate
    29	calls sharing one --now, the standalone validator (tools/
    30	bm_passport_validator.py) against the canonical fixture and the three

    33	repository) reports fields 2 and 5 as CARRIED from a deposited canonical
    34	passport. That last test is guarded: it skips, by name, when the sibling

   584	def _git(args, cwd, env=None):
   585	    full_env = dict(os.environ, GIT_AUTHOR_NAME="t", GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL="t@example.com",
   586	                    GIT_COMMITTER_NAME="t", GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL="t@example.com")

   593	
   594	def make_repo(tmp):
   595	    """A two commit repository at `tmp`. Returns (base, head)."""
   596	    _git(["init", "-q"], tmp)
   597	    write_text(tmp, "a.txt", "one\n")
   598	    _git(["add", "-A"], tmp)
   599	    _git(["commit", "-q", "-m", "one"], tmp)
   600	    base = _git(["rev-parse", "HEAD"], tmp).stdout.strip()
   601	    write_text(tmp, "a.txt", "two\n")
   602	    _git(["add", "-A"], tmp)
   603	    _git(["commit", "-q", "-m", "two"], tmp)
   604	    head = _git(["rev-parse", "HEAD"], tmp).stdout.strip()
   605	    return base, head
   606	
   607	
   608	def make_project_fixture(tmp, project_id="proj-1", actor_name="Fixture Person",
   609	                         session_id="sess-xyz", with_evidence=True):
   610	    """A real, healthy store at `tmp` carrying one project, one task, and
   611	    (unless with_evidence is False) one evidence row, all written through
   612	    bm_store.py's own write API, never a hand written store file. Every
   613	    write shares one actor, so the attribution log names one session."""
   614	    mod = _load_bm_store_module()
   615	    store = mod.Store(tmp, create=True)
   616	    actor = {"actor_type": "human", "actor_name": actor_name,
   617	             "session_id": session_id}
   618	    try:
   619	        store.upsert_project({
   620	            "project_id": project_id, "name": "Fixture Project",
   621	            "created_at": "2026-08-20T09:00:00Z",
   622	            "updated_at": "2026-08-20T09:00:00Z",
   623	        }, actor)
   624	        task_id = store.create_task({
   625	            "task_id": "task-1", "project_id": project_id,
   626	            "title": "Do the thing", "status": "planned",
   627	        }, actor)
   628	        if with_evidence:
   629	            store.add_evidence({
   630	                "evidence_id": "ev-1", "subject_type": "task",
   631	                "subject_id": task_id, "kind": "test",
   632	                "ref": "python3 tools/test_all.py", "note": "green",
   633	                "created_at": "2026-08-20T09:30:00Z",
   634	            }, project_id, actor)
   635	    finally:
   636	        store.close()
   637	    return task_id

 HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
 
+#: Written by the `generate` verb (see the section below build_deposit):
+#: the full five-field passport, schema/change-passport.v1.json's own
+#: shape, validated standalone by tools/bm_passport_validator.py. Distinct
+#: from the older no-verb "deposit" behavior above, which writes only the
+#: two fields execution provenance uniquely owns plus its own gap list.
+#: Kept side by side rather than merged: the deposit behavior above is
+#: already shipped, tested and wired into CI under its own CLI shape, and
+#: replacing it outright would drop a working, reviewed producer for an
+#: unproven rewrite. `generate` is additive; nothing about the no-verb
+#: path below changes.
+GENERATE_SCHEMA_MARKER = "change-passport/v1"
+
 #: Fixed by the contract: the producer always deposits here unless --out
 #: names a different path (used by this tool's own tests so they never
 #: touch a real .sbe/ directory).
@@ -368,6 +381,444 @@ def build_deposit(root, accountable_flag, method_flag):
     return deposit, not_established, notes
 
 
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+# `generate`: the full five-field passport (schema/change-passport.v1.json).
+#
+# Reads ONLY the five ReadOnlyStore accessors named in the task brief
+# (get_project, list_tasks, list_evidence, list_attribution,
+# open_key_decisions), plus git (subprocess, explicit failure paths) and
+# the --accountable / --method / --now flags. Never touches the fence
+# tables (records, claims) the no-verb deposit above reads through
+# store.dump(); "who did it" is rebuilt here from the attribution log
+# instead, which is one of the five allowed surfaces.
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+#: Default gap lines, one per keyword. A category is DROPPED only when that
+#: keyword (or its space-separated spelling) turns up in a task title or an
+#: evidence row's kind/ref/note, i.e. the store shows it was actually
+#: examined. This is a heuristic over free text, disclosed as one here and
+#: in docs/PASSPORT.md, not a claim of certainty.
+_DEFAULT_GAP_KEYWORDS = (
+    ("regression", "no regression pass is recorded in the store for this change"),
+    ("performance", "no performance measurement is recorded in the store for this change"),
+    ("cross-device", "no cross-device check is recorded in the store for this change"),
+    ("interface", "no interface behaviour review is recorded in the store for this change"),
+    ("translat", "the translated copy was not read by anyone who speaks the "
+                 "language, per this record"),
+)
+
+
+def _run_git(root, args):
+    """(stdout_or_None, error_or_None). Never raises: a missing git binary,
+    a bad ref, or a non-repository root all become a plain-English reason
+    string, never a traceback and never a silently empty result."""
+    try:
+        result = subprocess.run(
+            ["git", "-C", root] + list(args),
+            stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
+            text=True, encoding="utf-8", timeout=30)
+    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError) as e:
+        return None, "%s: %s" % (type(e).__name__, e)
+    if result.returncode != 0:
+        return None, (result.stderr.strip() or
+                      "git %s exited %d" % (" ".join(args), result.returncode))
+    return result.stdout, None
+
+
+def _repo_identity(root):
+    """The origin remote URL, or the directory name when there is none.
+    Never raises and never returns an empty string: a directory name is
+    always available as the last resort."""
+    out, _err = _run_git(root, ["remote", "get-url", "origin"])
+    if out and out.strip():
+        return out.strip()
+    return os.path.basename(os.path.abspath(root).rstrip(os.sep)) or root
+
+
+def _files_touched(root, base, head):
+    """(sorted_files, gap_reason_or_None). A git failure is reported as a
+    NAMED gap and an empty list, never a silent empty list with no
+    explanation (the design law this function exists to satisfy)."""
+    out, err = _run_git(root, ["diff", "--name-only", "%s..%s" % (base, head)])
+    if err is not None:
+        return [], ("git diff --name-only %s..%s failed: %s, so the files "
+                    "touched by this change could not be established"
+                    % (base, head, err))
+    files = sorted(set(line.strip() for line in out.splitlines() if line.strip()))
+    return files, None
+
+
+def _open_readonly_store(root):
+    """(store_or_None, state, reason). state is one of 'ok', 'absent',
+    'unreadable'. The caller is responsible for closing a returned store.
+    Mirrors _store_snapshot's three-state shape above so both verbs report
+    an absent or corrupt store the same honest way, without sharing code
+    that would otherwise open the store TWICE for one invocation."""
+    mod, err = _load_bm_store()
+    if mod is None:
+        return None, "unreadable", "bm_store.py could not be loaded (%s)" % err
+    store_path = mod.store_path(root)
+    try:
+        store = mod.ReadOnlyStore(root)
+    except mod.OwnershipRefused as e:
+        if e.reason == "no-store":
+            return None, "absent", "no BrotherMode store at %s" % store_path
+        return None, "unreadable", ("the BrotherMode store at %s could not "
+                                    "be opened: %s" % (store_path, e))
+    except mod.StoreCorrupt as e:
+        return None, "unreadable", ("the BrotherMode store at %s is "
+                                    "corrupt: %s" % (store_path, e))
+    except Exception as e:
+        return None, "unreadable", ("the BrotherMode store at %s could not "
+                                    "be opened: %s: %s"
+                                    % (store_path, type(e).__name__, e))
+    return store, "ok", None
+
+
+def _session_lines(attribution_rows, tasks_by_id):
+    """Every session named on the attribution log for this project, sorted
+    by session id, each with the claims (output artifacts, or else the
+    task it touched, or else the action name) it can be shown to hold.
+    Returns a list of {"label", "claims"} dicts, schema/change-
+    passport.v1.json's own shape for details.who.sessions."""
+    groups = {}
+    for row in attribution_rows:
+        sid = (row.get("session_id") or "").strip()
+        if not sid:
+            continue
+        claims = groups.setdefault(sid, set())
+        artifacts = [a for a in (row.get("output_artifacts") or []) if a]
+        if artifacts:
+            for artifact in artifacts:
+                claims.add(str(artifact))
+        else:
+            task_id = row.get("task_id")
+            if task_id and task_id in tasks_by_id:
+                claims.add(tasks_by_id[task_id].get("title") or task_id)
+            elif row.get("action"):
+                claims.add(str(row["action"]))
+    return [{"label": sid, "claims": sorted(groups[sid])} for sid in sorted(groups)]
+
+
+def _resolve_accountable(root, explicit, attribution_rows):
+    """(name_or_None, gap_reason_or_None). Three sources in order:
+    --accountable, the newest human actor on the attribution log (already
+    newest-first per list_attribution's own ORDER BY), `git config
+    user.name`. None for both means every source came up empty; the
+    caller turns that into the hard generation error the design names."""
+    if explicit and explicit.strip():
+        return explicit.strip(), None
+    for row in attribution_rows:
+        name = (row.get("actor_name") or "").strip()
+        if row.get("actor_type") == "human" and name:
+            return name, ("the accountable person was INFERRED from the "
+                          "newest human attribution event recorded for this "
+                          "project; no --accountable flag was given")
+    name = _git_user_name(root)
+    if name:
+        return name, ("the accountable person was INFERRED from `git "
+                      "config user.name` at %s, which resolves the "
+                      "machine's global git identity; no --accountable flag "
+                      "was given and the store named no human actor" % root)
+    return None, None
+
+
+def _method_name(method_flag, decision_rows):
+    """(name, was_defaulted). --method wins outright. Otherwise the newest
+    open DECISION insight whose decision_class is "method" (open_key_
+    decisions is already newest-first) supplies the name the store itself
+    recorded. Only the hard-coded default is used when neither exists."""
+    if method_flag and method_flag.strip():
+        return method_flag.strip(), False
+    for row in decision_rows:
+        if (row.get("decision_class") or "").strip().lower() == "method":
+            candidate = (row.get("subject") or row.get("claim") or "").strip()
+            if candidate:
+                return candidate, False
+    return "no method plugin detected; native flow", True
+
+
+def _evidence_entries(evidence_pairs):
+    """[(task_dict, entry_dict), ...] -> details.evidence entries. Origin
+    is always "local": the BrotherMode evidence table (kind/ref/note) has
+    no column recording whether a build system produced a check, unlike
+    BrotherSBE's own receipts (ciRunId). Reported honestly as a named gap
+    by the caller rather than guessed per entry."""
+    entries = []
+    for _task, row in evidence_pairs:
+        command = (row.get("ref") or row.get("kind") or "").strip() or "(unspecified check)"
+        verdict = (row.get("note") or "").strip() or "(no verdict recorded)"
+        entries.append({
+            "command": command,
+            "verdict": verdict,
+            "origin": "local",
+            "timestamp": row.get("created_at") or "",
+        })
+    return entries
+
+
+def _examined_text_blob(tasks, evidence_pairs):
+    parts = [str(t.get("title") or "") for t in tasks]
+    for _task, row in evidence_pairs:
+        parts.append(str(row.get("kind") or ""))
+        parts.append(str(row.get("ref") or ""))
+        parts.append(str(row.get("note") or ""))
+    return " ".join(parts).lower()
+
+
+def _default_gap_lines(tasks, evidence_pairs):
+    blob = _examined_text_blob(tasks, evidence_pairs)
+    lines = []
+    for keyword, sentence in _DEFAULT_GAP_KEYWORDS:
+        if keyword in blob or keyword.replace("-", " ") in blob:
+            continue
+        lines.append(sentence)
+    return lines
+
+
+def _canonical_now(now_arg):
+    """(iso_string_or_None, error_or_None). Canonicalises to seconds-
+    precision UTC ("...Z") whether the caller passed --now or not, so two
+    invocations with the SAME --now produce byte-identical output
+    regardless of the offset form given (a trailing Z, a numeric offset,
+    or none at all)."""
+    if now_arg:
+        text = now_arg
+        if text.endswith("Z"):
+            text = text[:-1] + "+00:00"
+        try:
+            parsed = datetime.datetime.fromisoformat(text)
+        except ValueError as e:
+            return None, "invalid --now value %r: %s" % (now_arg, e)
+        if parsed.tzinfo is None:
+            parsed = parsed.replace(tzinfo=datetime.timezone.utc)
+        parsed = parsed.astimezone(datetime.timezone.utc)
+    else:
+        parsed = datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc)
+    return parsed.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"), None
+
+
+def build_passport(root, project_id, base, head, accountable_flag, method_flag,
+                   now_arg):
+    """(passport_dict_or_None, error_or_None). error set means generation
+    refused outright (only the empty-accountable law does this); every
+    other gap is folded into details.notEstablished / whatWasNotEstablished
+    instead, per the design's "reports, does not block" rule."""
+    now_str, now_err = _canonical_now(now_arg)
+    if now_err:
+        return None, now_err
+
+    repo = _repo_identity(root)
+    files, files_gap = _files_touched(root, base, head)
+
+    store, state, reason = _open_readonly_store(root)
+    tasks, attribution, decisions, evidence_pairs = [], [], [], []
+    store_gap = None
+    if state == "ok":
+        try:
+            # raw=True on every one of the five allowed accessors, on
+            # purpose: their default (raw=False) applies bm_store.py's own
+            # founder-text scrubber (_export_row), which withholds free text
+            # as "[WITHHELD: N chars of founder text]". That is the right
+            # default for an untrusted dump, but a passport's whole job is
+            # to carry REAL session labels, task titles and check commands;
+            # a redacted placeholder in whoDidIt or whatWasRun is not a
+            # privacy win, it is a passport that lies by looking filled.
+            # Precedent: the no-verb deposit path above already reads
+            # store.dump(raw=True) for the identical reason, and raw=True
+            # opens "no new disclosure surface beyond what dump(raw=True)
+            # already grants" per _export_row's own docstring.
+            project = store.get_project(project_id, raw=True)
+            tasks = store.list_tasks(project_id, raw=True)
+            attribution = store.list_attribution(project_id, limit=500, raw=True)
+            decisions = store.open_key_decisions(project_id, raw=True)
+            for task in tasks:
+                for row in store.list_evidence("task", task["task_id"], raw=True):
+                    evidence_pairs.append((task, row))
+            if project is None:
+                store_gap = ("no project %r was found in the BrotherMode "
+                             "store, so its tasks, evidence and decisions "
+                             "could not be read" % project_id)
+        finally:
+            store.close()
+    else:
+        store_gap = "%s, so session, evidence and decision history for this change could not be read" % reason
+
+    tasks_by_id = {t["task_id"]: t for t in tasks}
+    sessions = _session_lines(attribution, tasks_by_id)
+    accountable_name, accountable_gap = _resolve_accountable(
+        root, accountable_flag, attribution)
+    if accountable_name is None:
+        return None, ("no accountable human could be established: no "
+                      "--accountable flag was given, the store named no "
+                      "human actor, and `git config user.name` returned "
+                      "nothing at %s" % root)
+
+    method_name, method_defaulted = _method_name(method_flag, decisions)
+    evidence_entries = _evidence_entries(evidence_pairs)
+
+    items = list(_default_gap_lines(tasks, evidence_pairs))
+    for gap in (files_gap, store_gap, accountable_gap):
+        if gap:
+            items.append(gap)
+    if evidence_entries:
+        items.append(
+            "evidence origin (build system vs laptop) is not tracked by "
+            "the BrotherMode store's own evidence table; every entry above "
+            "is reported as local until that distinction exists")
+    justification = None
+    if not items:
+        justification = ("every default gap category was found examined in "
+                         "the store's own task and evidence text, and no "
+                         "other gap was detected by this generator")
+
+    what_was_done = ["change in %s, commit range %s..%s" % (repo, base, head)]
+    if files:
+        what_was_done.append(
+            "%d file(s) touched: %s" % (len(files), ", ".join(files)))
+    else:
+        what_was_done.append(
+            "no files were touched between %s and %s" % (base, head))
+
+    who_did_it = ["accountable: %s" % accountable_name]
+    for session in sessions:
+        claims = ", ".join(session["claims"]) if session["claims"] else "(no claim named)"
+        who_did_it.append("session %s: claims %s" % (session["label"], claims))
+
+    if evidence_entries:
+        what_was_run = [
+            "`%s` returned %s (%s, %s)" % (
+                e["command"], e["verdict"], e["origin"], e["timestamp"])
+            for e in evidence_entries]
+    else:
+        what_was_run = [
+            "no verification evidence is recorded in the BrotherMode store "
+            "for project %r" % project_id]
+
+    where_it_came_from = [method_name]
+    what_was_not_established = items if items else [justification]
+
+    passport = {
+        "schema": GENERATE_SCHEMA_MARKER,
+        "generatedAt": now_str,
+        "whatWasDone": what_was_done,
+        "whoDidIt": who_did_it,
+        "whatWasRun": what_was_run,
+        "whatWasNotEstablished": what_was_not_established,
+        "whereItCameFrom": where_it_came_from,
+        "change": {
+            "repo": repo,
+            "baseCommit": base,
+            "headCommit": head,
+            "filesTouched": files,
+        },
+        "details": {
+            "who": {"sessions": sessions, "accountableHuman": accountable_name},
+            "evidence": evidence_entries,
+            "method": {"name": method_name},
+            "notEstablished": {"items": items, "noneClaimJustification": justification},
+        },
+    }
+    return passport, None
+
+
+def _usage_generate():
+    cmd = None
+    mod, _err = _load_bm_store()
+    if mod is not None:
+        try:
+            cmd = mod.invocation("bm_passport.py", __file__)
+        except Exception:
+            cmd = None
+    if not cmd:
+        cmd = "python3 %s" % os.path.abspath(__file__)
+    return ("Usage: %s generate --project-id ID --base SHA --head SHA\n"
+            "         [--accountable NAME] [--method NAME] [--now ISO8601]\n"
+            "         [--out PATH] [--root DIR]\n\n"
+            "Writes the full five-field change passport (schema/change-"
+            "passport.v1.json)\nto stdout, or to --out when given. Exit 0 "
+            "on a written passport, whatever it\ncould and could not "
+            "establish. Exit 1 when no accountable human could be\n"
+            "established, or when --out could not be written. Exit 2 for a "
+            "usage error\nor an unresolvable project root." % cmd)
+
+
+def _parse_generate_argv(argv):
+    """(args_dict_or_None, error_or_None). error == 'help' means print
+    generate usage and exit 0."""
+    values = {"project_id": None, "base": None, "head": None,
+             "accountable": None, "method": None, "now": None,
+             "out": None, "root": None}
+    flag_to_key = {
+        "--project-id": "project_id", "--base": "base", "--head": "head",
+        "--accountable": "accountable", "--method": "method",
+        "--now": "now", "--out": "out", "--root": "root",
+    }
+    args = list(argv)
+    i = 0
+    while i < len(args):
+        arg = args[i]
+        if arg in ("--help", "-h"):
+            return None, "help"
+        if arg in flag_to_key:
+            if i + 1 >= len(args):
+                return None, "bm_passport generate: %s requires a value" % arg
+            values[flag_to_key[arg]] = args[i + 1]
+            i += 2
+        else:
+            return None, "bm_passport generate: unknown argument: %s" % arg
+    missing = [flag for flag, key in
+              (("--project-id", "project_id"), ("--base", "base"), ("--head", "head"))
+              if not (values[key] or "").strip()]
+    if missing:
+        return None, ("bm_passport generate: missing required argument(s): "
+                      "%s" % ", ".join(missing))
+    return values, None
+
+
+def _run_generate(argv):
+    values, err = _parse_generate_argv(argv)
+    if err == "help":
+        sys.stdout.write(_usage_generate() + "\n")
+        return 0
+    if err:
+        sys.stderr.write(err + "\n")
+        sys.stderr.write(_usage_generate() + "\n")
+        return 2
+
+    root, reason = resolve_project_root(values["root"])
+    if root is None:
+        sys.stdout.write("NO-DATA: %s\n" % reason)
+        return 2
+
+    passport, error = build_passport(
+        root, values["project_id"], values["base"], values["head"],
+        values["accountable"], values["method"], values["now"])
+    if error:
+        sys.stderr.write("bm-passport generate: %s\n" % error)
+        return 1
+
+    text = json.dumps(passport, sort_keys=True, indent=2,
+                      ensure_ascii=False) + "\n"
+    if not values["out"]:
+        sys.stdout.write(text)
+        return 0
+    try:
+        out_path = values["out"]
+        parent = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(out_path))
+        if parent and not os.path.isdir(parent):
+            os.makedirs(parent)
+        tmp_path = out_path + ".tmp"
+        with open(tmp_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
+            fh.write(text)
+        os.replace(tmp_path, out_path)
+    except OSError as e:
+        sys.stderr.write("bm-passport generate: could not write %s (%s)\n"
+                         % (values["out"], e))
+        return 1
+    return 0
+
+
 # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 # CLI.
 # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
@@ -501,6 +952,14 @@ def _run(argv):
 
 def main(argv):
     try:
+        # "generate" is a new, additive verb (see build_passport above): the
+        # full five-field passport, a different CLI shape from the no-verb
+        # deposit behavior below it. Any OTHER first argument, flag or bare
+        # word, falls through to the original parser unchanged, which is
+        # what already refuses a stray positional argument as "unknown
+        # argument"; no existing invocation shape changes.
+        if argv and argv[0] == "generate":
+            return _run_generate(argv[1:])
         return _run(argv)
     except Exception as exc:
         sys.stdout.write("NO-DATA: %s: %s\n" % (type(exc).__name__, exc))

+ADDED: the `generate` verb (schema/change-passport.v1.json), a second,
+additive CLI shape on the same tool that writes the full five field
+passport rather than the three field deposit above. Its own section below
